Questão 1

According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements and mark true (T) or false (F): (Glossary terms) ( ) Breasted/alongside towing: A tug securely lashed alongside a ship, usually with a minimum of two lines: head line and spring line or stern line. Also called 'on the hip' or 'hipped up'. ( ) Box keel: An enclosed keel structure extending from the aft skeg (if fitted) to a point close to the forefoot of a tug. A box keel is sometimes installed on Tractor escort tugs to provide a better course stability on astern and additional lift forces, resulting in higher towing forces, when operating as stern tug in the indirect towing mode. A Box keel gives additional strength to the tug's hull and provides a better distribution of dock forces when in dry-dock. ( ) Cross lines/gate lines: Separate lines from either side of the tow to the opposite quarter of the tug or the opposite side of the tug's H-towing bitt.

A) (F) (F) (T)

B) (F) (T) (F)

C) (F) (T) (T)

D) (T) (F) (F)

E) (T) (T) (T)

Questão 4

Consider the following statements regarding to “Course stability and directional stability”: (Glossary terms)
I. Course stability is also called directional stability, stability of route or dynamic stability of route.
II. Course stability is that property of a ship (which includes tugs) that, when disturbed, damps out extraneous motions set up by the disturbance and to reduce them progressively to zero.
III. Course stability should not be confused with dynamic stability, which is, strictly speaking, the ability of a ship to follow a certain direction, e.g. by means of an automatic steering system.
IV. A ship closely following a selected heading has directional stability but may be course unstable, which results in frequent rudder (or thruster) actions to hold the ship on its course. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I) and (II) solely are true

B) All of them are true

C) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

D) (II) and (IV) solely are true

E) (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

Questão 20

A) A força de tração estática a ré é, geralmente, bastante inferior à força de tração a vante.

B) São os rebocadores de concepção mais antiga ainda em atividade, podendo ser dotados de um ou mais hélices, sempre fixos.

C) A posição do ponto de tração principal, geralmente um gato de escape a ré, é sempre a uma distância, ante a ré do propulsor, que permita o governo quando em operação com cabo no gato.

D) Da mesma forma que os rebocadores de um hélice, a utilização de rebocadores de dois ou mais hélices com cabo passado na popa do navio com seguimento a vante é desaconselhável e arriscada, se houver necessidade de uma mudança de bordo.

E) Têm uma capacidade de manobra limitada e sua utilização requer atenção constante nas situações que possam comprometer sua estabilidade.

Questão 27

Considere as afirmativas abaixo relacionadas aos Rebocadores com propulsão a vante (Tratores): (Cap.1, p.21)
I. Tem, normalmente, o ponto de aplicação da força de tração (guincho, cabeço ou gato) na popa. Assim, com o propulsor a vante e o cabo de reboque na popa, o rebocador trator tem excelente atuação trabalhando com cabo da proa do navio.
II. Os propulsores, sempre em número de dois, são dispostos em linha, equidistantes do plano diametral, a vante da embarcação. Em geral, podem navegar para ré com a mesma desenvoltura que para vante, elegendo o sentido da movimentação em função da posição onde vão operar, já que o cabo deve ser passado sempre a ré do rebocador.
III. Para operar com cabo na popa ou no costado, a aproximação será sempre de proa.
IV. Para operar na proa, o rebocador navega no sentido normal, de proa, evitando, mesmo em alta velocidade, graças às características e posição dos propulsores, os efeitos da interação, enquanto passa ou recebe o cabo do navio. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), analise as afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta:

A) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

B) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

C) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (III) são verdadeiras.

D) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (II) são verdadeiras.

E) Todas as afirmativas são verdadeiras.

Questão 29

(PROVA PSPP 2012) De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), analise as afirmativas abaixo e assinale a opção INCORRETA: (Cap.1, p.22-27)

A) Em relação aos rebocadores tratores, os tratores reversos e os ASD, devido à localização dos propulsores na popa, correm menor risco de ter esses propulsores atingidos em caso de colisão ou encalhe, assim como operam com menores calados.

B) O método de assistência/utilização com cabo de reboque passado na proa do navio é especialmente favorável à atuação dos rebocadores do tipo tratores, pois, tendo os propulsores a vante, conseguem se aproximar da proa do navio.

C) A principal vantagem dos rebocadores cicloidais (Voith Schneider), além de poderem atuar para vante ou para a ré com a mesma força de tração, é a velocidade com que as alterações do sentido da aplicação e intensidade da força podem ser realizadas.

D) Normalmente, os rebocadores azimutais com propulsão a vante possuem maior calado que os rebocadores cicloidais, não sendo, portanto, indicados para manobras em águas de pouca profundidade.

E) Os rebocadores azimutais tipo ASD compartilham as qualidades dos tratores reversos com as dos rebocadores convencionais, o que lhes permite grande flexibilidade na forma de atuação.

Questão 30

Este tipo de rebocador apresenta diferenças quanto aos tratores reversos azimutais no posicionamento da superestrutura, no espaço de convés disponível a ré e na posição do gato, em relação à posição dos propulsores. Nestes aspectos, assemelham-se mais a um rebocador convencional, tendo comportamento semelhante quando atuando com cabo passado em sua popa. Compartilha as qualidades do trator reverso com as do rebocador convencional, permitindo grande flexibilidade na forma de atuação. Tem sido a opção de construção mais comum entre os azimutais. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), no Brasil , em particular, a grande maioria dos rebocadores azimutais construídos é classificada como: (Cap.1, p.27-28)

Questão 32

De uma maneira geral, os rebocadores são necessários nas seguintes situações: reboque, atracação ou desatracação, auxílio no governo ou giro do navio e acompanhamento (escort). Para operar em quaisquer destas situações, os rebocadores podem ser utilizados com cabo de reboque da proa ou popa do navio, no costado ou uma combinação entre os dois métodos. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), analise as afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta: (Cap.2, p.31-32)
I. Neste método o cabo sai da proa ou popa do navio pela buzina central ou próxima ao centro. Também conhecido como “cabo longo” ou “método europeu” (on a hawser).
II. A Força gerada estará na extremidade (com maior braço de alavanca) e a maior tração transversal será conseguida a 90o em relação ao eixo diametral do navio.
III. O ângulo que o rebocador mantém em relação à corrente ou ao rumo do navio vai influenciar fortemente a força de tração que consegue aplicar. Quanto maior o ângulo do casco do rebocador em relação à corrente, menor será a força gasta para manter o rebocador na posição e menor será a força útil para uso na manobra.
IV. Tanto com o cabo na proa quanto na popa do navio, o rebocador aumentará sua força de tração efetiva, ficando o mais alinhado possível com a corrente.

A) Todas as afirmativas são verdadeiras.

B) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (III) são verdadeiras.

C) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

D) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (II) são verdadeiras.

Questão 33

O rebocador na popa do navio, é o melhor ponto de aplicação da força quando se quer manobrar um navio com problemas de governo. É, também, uma utilização clássica para os rebocadores azimutais ou cicloidais com propulsão a ré. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), analise as afirmativas relacionadas à Ação Direta e marque verdadeiro (V) ou falso (F): (Cap.2, p.37) ( ) É a forma mais “natural” e mais utilizada. ( ) O rebocador irá, simplesmente, puxar, prolongando o cabo e levando sua extremidade livre na direção que quer a aplicação da força, atuando para governar e para criar ou reduzir uma tendência do navio para um dos bordos. Entretanto, não pode ser utilizada para quebrar o seguimento. ( ) A extremidade de trabalho de um rebocador será onde se localizar o ponto de aplicação da força de tração (proa ou popa) e vai depender de suas características. A outra extremidade é a extremidade livre. ( ) Na ação direta, ao aumentar a velocidade do navio, o rebocador levará menos tempo para se posicionar e atuar em cada um dos bordos, quando se quer governar o navio. ( ) É, normalmente, o método mais utilizado quando os navios estão parados ou em baixa velocidade.

A) (F) (V) (F) (V) (F)

B) (V) (F) (V) (F) (V)

Questão 35

(PROVA PSPP 2008) De acordo com Fragoso & Cajaty, no livro “Rebocadores Portuários”, o que é arrasto transverso? (Cap.2, p.39)

A) é quando um rebocador, com cabo de reboque pela popa de um navio, se posiciona transversalmente ao movimento do navio.

B) é quando um rebocador azimutal, com cabo passado na popa de um navio com seguimento avante, direciona um propulsor para cada bordo.

C) é quando os rebocadores empurram o costado do navio, arrastando-o transversalmente ao seu comprimento.

D) é quando o rebocador, atuando no método “puxa-empurra”, é arrastado transversalmente pelo segmento do navio.

E) é quando os rebocadores de proa e de popa atuam transversalmente (um para cada bordo) ao segmento do navio, fazendo-o girar.

Questão 36

O deslocamento do navio é a característica mais óbvia com base na qual se pode calcular, através de um modelo matemático, o bollard pull necessário para mover um navio. Questão 37

Podem ser consideradas na avaliação do bollard pull requerido para manobrar um navio sob ação de vento três diferentes possibilidades: 1) a força produzida pela ação do vento lateral, assim considerado o de ângulo de incidência inferior a trinta graus em relação à normal; 2) a força produzida pela ação de vento longitudinal (de proa ou popa); e 3) a força de torção cujo maior efeito se dá com os ventos que incidem na bochecha ou alheta do navio e varia com o formato do navio e com o trim. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), é na primeira possibilidade que são produzidas forças de maior intensidade e, consequentemente, de maior impacto sobre as manobras. A fórmula mais utilizada para o cálculo da força do vento lateral é a seguinte: (Cap.3, p.51)

A) Flat = 0.5. Clat. r. V2. Alat

B) Flat = 0.031. V2. Lbp.T

C) Flat = 0.00008. Alat (m2). V2 (m/s)

D) Flat = 0.15. V2. Lbp.T

E) Flat = 0.5. Clat. r. V2. Lbp.T

Questão 39

As forças produzidas pela corrente, da mesma forma que a produzida pelo vento, vai variar em função da área do navio exposta à sua incidência. Assim, para navios de igual comprimento, quanto maior o calado maior a força gerada pela ação da corrente. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), considere as seguintes afirmativas e assinale a alternativa correta: (Cap.3, p.51-52)

A) Também muito importante na avaliação da força produzida pela corrente é a relação entre a folga sob a quilha e o calado do navio. Quanto menor esta relação, maior a força exercida pela corrente. De acordo com resultados de testes realizados em tanques com modelos reduzidos de navios, a força da corrente dobra quando reduzimos a relação folga sob a quilha/calado de 1,6 para 0,2.

B) Também muito importante na avaliação da força produzida pela corrente é a relação entre a folga sob a quilha e o calado do navio. Quanto menor esta relação, menor a força exercida pela corrente. De acordo com resultados de testes realizados em tanques com modelos reduzidos de navios, a força da corrente triplica quando reduzimos a relação folga sob a quilha/calado de 1,6 para 0,2.

C) Também muito importante na avaliação da força produzida pela corrente é a relação entre a folga sob a quilha e o calado do navio. Quanto menor esta relação, menor a força exercida pela corrente. De acordo com resultados de testes realizados em tanques com modelos reduzidos de navios, a força da corrente dobra quando reduzimos a relação folga sob a quilha/calado de 1,6 para 0,2.

D) Também muito importante na avaliação da força produzida pela corrente é a relação entre a folga sob a quilha e o calado do navio. Quanto menor esta relação, maior a força exercida pela corrente. De acordo com resultados de testes realizados em tanques com modelos reduzidos de navios, a força da corrente quadruplica quando reduzimos a relação folga sob a quilha/calado de 1,6 para 0,2.

E) Também muito importante na avaliação da força produzida pela corrente é a relação entre a folga sob a quilha e o calado do navio. Quanto menor esta relação, maior a força exercida pela corrente. De acordo com resultados de testes realizados em tanques com modelos reduzidos de navios, a força da corrente triplica quando reduzimos a relação folga sob a quilha/calado de 1,6 para 0,2.

Questão 40

A fórmula mais utilizada para o cálculo do bollard pull necessária para controlar um navio sob ação de corrente é a desenvolvida pela OCIMF. Da mesma forma que as fórmulas para o cálculo da força gerada pelo vento, são consideradas três situações de incidência da corrente: 1) corrente incidindo perpendicularmente ao plano diametral do navio ou em ângulos inferiores a trinta graus em relação à normal (transversal); 2) corrente incidindo longitudinalmente (proa ou popa); e 3) força de torção produzida, principalmente, por corrente incidindo na direção das alhetas ou bochechas ou pelas diferenças no formato das obras vivas a vante e a ré do navio. De uma forma geral, o maior impacto será o causado pela corrente transversal. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), assinale a fórmula da OClMF com a simplificação propasta por Hensen em Tug use in Port para a força da corrente lateral: (Cap.3, p.52)

C) Fcl = 0.031. V2 . Lbp. T

D) Fcl = 0.00008. A (m2). V2 (m/s)

E) Fcl = 0.15. V2 . Lbp. T

Questão 47

O maior problema dos lemes convencionais é a formação de turbulências quando o ângulo de carregamento é muito alto, que levam o leme a "estolar” e perder o efeito. Tal quadro é especialmente grave em situações de manobra quando a velocidade é sempre reduzida e necessitamos de maiores ângulos de leme para garantir a manobrabilidade do navio. Visando resolver esta dificuldade, foram desenvolvidos lemes com características especiais que mantivessem a efetividade do leme mesmo com ângulos de carregamento bastante altos. Entre os lemes dotados de flap móvel na saída d'água (Becker, Jastram e Ulster), o modelo mais conhecido é o Ieme Becker. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), analise as afirmativas relacionadas ao leme Becker, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção incorreta: (Cap.5, p.76)

A) No leme Becker, menores ângulos de leme têm ângulos de flap proporcionalmente maiores.

B) Com qualquer ângulo de leme, o fluxo de água passa suavemente pela superfície do leme, sem formação de turbulências que afetam sua eficiência. Como efeito, o navio tem suas qualidades evolutivas bastante aumentadas.

C) Com o ângulo máximo de carregamento do leme (aproximadamente 50 graus) o efeito na redução da velocidade é acentuado.

D) A particularidade do leme Becker em relação ao leme convencional é a existência do flap com área correspondente a 20% ou 30% da área total do leme.

E) É normalmente governado junto com a parte principal do leme, variando seu ângulo de 1,5 a 2 vezes o ângulo dado na parte principal. Isso significa que se o leme é carregado para 30 graus a BE, por exemplo, o flap automaticamente apresentará um ângulo de aproximadamente 60 graus.

Questão 48

O Leme Cicloidal ou Voith Cicloidal Rudder (VCR) é uma inovação, lançada pelo mesmo fabricante dos propulsores Voith-Schneider, que tem encontrado bastante aceitação pela simplicidade do conceito. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), analise as afirmativas relacionadas ao leme Cicloidal (VCR) e marque verdadeiro (V) ou falso (F): (Cap.5, p.78-79) ( ) Como no propulsor cicloidal, o VCR tem um disco colocado paralelamente ao fundo do navio e deste disco saem duas lâminas. Todo o conjunto é localizado por ante a ré do propulsor, recebendo a sua corrente de descarga. ( ) O leme cicloidal pode trabalhar de duas formas: passiva ou ativa. No modo passivo, o rotor apenas gira parcialmente, para um lado ou para o outro, como faria uma porta (dupla) de leme convencional e é usado com o navio em baixa velocidade. ( ) Quando a velocidade do navio é alta o leme VCR passa a atuar no modo ativo. O disco começa a rodar em velocidade uniforme e, como no propulsor cicloidal, as lâminas passam a variar seu passo de forma a produzir a força na direção desejada. ( ) No modo ativo, o leme cicloidal não pode atuar aumentando o seguimento, apenas reduzindo-o em conjunto com o propulsor principal, ou atuar como um stern-thruster com controle direcional da força aplicada. ( ) São vantagens adicionais do leme cicloidal sobre o leme convencional a menor área de atrito e a maior eficiência.

A) (V) (F) (V) (F) (V)

B) (V) (F) (F) (V) (F)

C) (V) (F) (F) (F) (V)

D) (F) (V) (F) (V) (F)

E) (F) (V) (V) (F) (V)

Questão 49

Alguns navios são dotados de equipamentos ou recebem aperfeiçoamentos com o objetivo de aumentar sua capacidade de manobra, permitindo uma reavaliação do número de rebocadores utilizados nas manobras portuárias ou mesmo eliminando sua necessidade. De acordo com o contido no livro “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso e Marcelo Cajaty - 1ª edição: 2002), analise as afirmativas relacionados a navios com recursos especiais, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção incorreta: (Cap.5, p.73-78)

A) A principal crítica ao controlable pitch propeller prende-se à dificuldade de governo quando se “pára a máquina”, isto é, quando o hélice fica com passo zero. Nesta situação, o hélice “fechado” não permite a descarga de água no leme, acabando com o escoamento e as qualidades hidrodinâmicas que dão governo ao navio.

B) A eficiência dos lemes especiais, como por exemplo, no tipo de leme Schilling, depende basicamente da adequação entre o tamanho do leme e o do propulsor e a proporcionalidade entre a área do leme e as dimensões do navio.

C) A melhor atuação dos thrusters (propulsores transversais) ocorre com o navio parado, reduzindo-se a eficiência drasticamente conforme aumenta a velocidade. Com cinco nós de velocidade, a maioria dos thrusters perde significativamente sua eficácia, em função da combinação da força exercida lateralmente com o fluxo de água derivado do movimento longitudinal produzir uma resultante quase paralela ao costado do navio.

D) O leme cicloidal (VCR) pode trabalhar de duas formas: passiva ou ativa. Quando a velocidade do navio é baixa e o leme começa a perder eficiência, o VCR passa a atuar no modo passivo. O disco começa a rodar em velocidade uniforme e, como no propulsor cicloidal, as lâminas passam a variar seu passo de forma a produzir a força na direção desejada.

E) Navios equipados com azipod são mais silenciosos, consomem menos combustível e ganham espaços normalmente destinados à casa de máquinas, tubo do eixo etc.

Questão 50

Consider the following statements regarding to OCIMF - “Recommendations For Ships' Fittings For Use With Tugs – With Particular Reference to Escorting and Other High Load Operations”, (Towing Pactices - Escorting): (Anexo 4, p.111)
I. Escorting is becoming a widely used method of risk management at various ports and harbours worldwide. It is exclusively used for tankers. It differs from the normal use of harbour tugs in that the escort tug is in attendance and may be made fast (active or tethered escorting) for an extended distance, with the escorted vessel making way at speeds of typically 5 to 12 knots.
II. The usual purpose of escorting is to assist the tanker in the event of steering and/or propulsion failure. Within the limits of tug safety, the Escort tug can take the way off the tanker, alter its heading or both. Crucially, it can slow the tanker down so that it becomes easier to control.
III. It is importanr to note that with the escorted vessel underway, and the tug applying a towing force at an angle to the ship's heading, the water flow against the hull and the tug's own displacement will generate hydrodynamic forces. When combined with the propulsion forces these may give a resultant (steering) force in the towline far exceeding the static bollard pull of the tug. According to Otávio Fragoso & Marcelo Cajaty, in the book “Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA”, what is the correct alternative?

A) (I) and (II) solely are true

B) None of them are true

C) All of them are true

D) (I) and (III) solely are true

E) (II) and (III) solely are true

Questão 51

According to the book ““Rebocadores Portuários - CONAPRA” (Otávio Fragoso & Marcelo Cajaty – 1st Ed.: 2002), choose the incorrect statement regarding to OCIMF - “Recommendations For Ships' Fittings For Use With Tugs – With Particular Reference to Escorting and Other High Load Operations”, (Station Keeping at Offshore Installations - Pull-back): (Anexo 4, p.111-112)

A) A different phenomenon occurs at Single Point Moorings (SPMs) where the offtake tanker connects directly to the SPM and weathervanes around it. When connected to the SPM, the tanker will normally lie to the resultant force of wind and current with slight tension on the mooring hawser(s) and clear of the SPM. On occasion, due to a particular combination of wind and current, the tanker may ride up to the buoy with a consequent risk of contact and damage to both. One possible solution in such cases is to connect a tug to the stern of the tanker during such periods to maintain a tension in the mooring hawser and to keep the tanker bow clear of the SPM.

B) One of the principal features of most offshore installations is that the offtake vessel weathervanes. If the installation is a turret moored Floating (Production) Storage and Offloading Unit (F(P)SO), the of loading tanker can still weathervane but its behaviour is influenced by, and coupled to, that of the f(P)SO. Depending on their relative loading conditions, the F(P)SO and the offloading tanker may assume widely differing headings with respect to the prevailing environmental conditions.

C) When vessels are stable on station, the phenomenon of fishtailing don’t develop. Fishtailing occurs when the bow of the offtake tanker remains in approximately the same position relative to the stern of the F(P)SO, but the stern moves from side to side so that the vessels no longer lie in a straight line. If the stern of the offtake tanker moves outside the predetermined safe envelope then the movement needs to be corrected. One solution in such cases is to connect a standby vessel to the stern of the offtake tanker to pull the ship back within limits and then hold it there.

D) Some F(P)Sos are spread moored so that the installation is fixed in azimuth. In these circumstances the offtake tanker cannot weathervane and powerfull "pull-back" tugs are essential to maintain alignment between thce vessels during the approach manoeuvre and during cargo transfer. During these operations very highr towline forces may be applied to the offtake ranker.

E) Tugs used at offshore locations are often multi-purpose vessels used for anchor handling, remotely operated vehicle (ROV) work and other production field functions. Tanker owners and Masters should be aware that multipurpose tugs are not always the most suitable for ship handling and may have a bollard pull capability higher than strictly necessary for "pull-back" assistance.

Questão 54

In addition to the standard information passed to the Pilot, it is recommended that the Master provide the Pilot with a deck General Arrangement showing the layout and SWL of the mooring fittings and inform him. Questão 64

Com relação aos requisitos para um bom desempenho e segurança de um rebocador (Tug performance and safety), podemos afirmar que: (Cap.2, p.11)
I. Not only is a short response time required when assisting a vessel, but also for making fast. Due to ever decreasing numbers in a ship’s crew, the time taken to make tugs fast is increasing. Thus the requirement for tugs regarding fast and easy handling of towing equipments becomes an element of increasing importance in order to improve their response time.
II. It is not only manoeuvrability, but also bollard pull and underwater shape that make a tug effective and therefore suitable for the job.
III. When a ship is underway at speed, loss of tug's effectiveness due to the ship's speed and/or towing direction should be as small as possible. The effectiveness and safety of a tug is also related to factors such as the tug's stability and suitability of towing equipment.
IV. The manoeuvring space required by assiting tugs should, depending on the situation, be as small as possible. This can be achieved by good tug manoeuvrability, limited tug dimensions and proper towing equipment. De acordo com o contido no livro “Tug use in Port, a Practical Guide” (Captain Henk Hensen - 4ª edição: 2021), analise as afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta:

A) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (II) são verdadeiras.

B) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

C) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (III) são verdadeiras.

D) Todas as afirmativas são verdadeiras.

E) Apenas as afirmativas (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras.

Questão 65

When making fast to a vessel and while assisting, a tug captain should be able, to see in one glance from his manoeuvring station, the most essential outside information needed to operate his tug in a safe and efficient way, without jumping from side to side in his wheelhouse and without getting painful legs, neck or back. The essential outside information comes from: (Cap.2, p.11)
I. The towline(s) – their direction and tension.
II. The assisted ship – such as relative heading and speed, distance off and the way the assisted ship reacts to applied forces. When pushing, essential information also comes from the contact area between tug and ship.
III. The combined ship/tug direction of movement with regard to channel or fairway boundaries, other traffic and nearby berths and banks. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

A) (II) and (III) solely are true

B) All of them are true

C) (I) and (III) solely are true

D) None of them are true

E) (I) and (II) solely are true

Questão 66

Various systems are developed and/or introduced intended to support the tug master. A very welcome development. Daily practice is the real test of such systems whereby such questions as mentioned below are of value to verify if or to what extent these systems support a tug master. Focus should then not only be on the standard manoeuvres but in particular on the more critical situations a tug master has to deal with. Five crucial questions regarding the development and introduction of decision support systems for a tug master are: - Does it affect the tug master’s workload? - Does it distract the tug master’s eye from ‘out of the window’ visual clues which remain Paramount for manoeuvring alertly, especially in the more critical situations? - Does the decision support system indeed support the tug master in critical situations and adverse weather conditions, such as in case of operating near the bow of a ship at speed or in reduced visibility? - Does the decision support system warn the tug máster if a critical situation with respect to speed, direction, rate of turn, distances off, etc. develops and tells him to take action and what actions he has to take? - Does the system inform the tug master about the level of the tug’s stability and safe heeling angels limits? According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), this system which monitors a 360º field of view around the tugi s studied by Mitsui OSK lines and Oki Electric Industry. Four fish-eye cameras were installed on the tug Asaka Maru with a digital compositing system and display on the tug’s bridge. The system was able to automatically calculate the distance to nearby obstacles and other vessels: (Cap.2, p.13)

A) Raymarine DockSense Alert

B) Computational Dynamics Data

C) FlyingView bird’s eye view

D) Augmented technology (AR)

E) Damen Human Machine Interface

Questão 67

Tugs should be equipped with good fendering. Appropriate fendering protects both the assisted ship and tug from damage and decreases the tendency to slide along the ship's hull when the tug is pushing at an angle to the ship's hull. Fenders are constructed of rubber or synthetic rubber products. Beyond the mechanical requirements of load versus deflection and energy absorption, which is given in curves, attachment methods and structural limits, consideration should also be given to the material used in the fender. The material used should have good resistance to: (Cap.2, p.14)
I. polluted water
II. Ozone
III. UV radiation
IV. high and low temperatures According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (II) and (III) solely are true

B) All of them are true

C) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

D) (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

E) (I) and (II) solely are true

Questão 68

The following factors are of importance in the choice of a tug's bow and/ or stem fendering: (Cap.2, p.14)
I. The way the tug is assisting vessels, for instance towing on a line or push-pull, and whether the tug will push by the stern and/ or by the bow.
II. The size and engine power of the tug which are important factors for the horizontal load and kinetic energy transmitted during contact and pushing; and, the size of contact area.
III. The type and size of vessels to be handled eg ships with large bow flare and/ or overhanging stern. Tugs pushing near the bow or stern of these ships may need extra fendering on top of the bow to prevent damage to tug or ship.
IV. The environmental conditions such as waves and swell. These conditions will give rise to additional forces in the fendering, for which it must be able to compensate.
V. The tug's bow and stern construction. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (III), (IV) and (V) solely are true

B) (I) and (III) solely are true

C) (II) and (III) solely are true

D) (I), (III) and (V) solely are true

E) All of them are true

E) When ahead thrust is applied with port or starboard helm, the tug’s stern moves in the same direction to the intended direction of turn due to the aft location of the propeller and rudder.

The turning moment of the propellers is eliminated by the action of the wheel and the tug moves sideways. Propeller effectiveness is less on astern therefore ahead pitch should be set somewhat lower than astern pitch.
III. VS tug propulsion produces little wash, which is invaluable when skimming oil and, for example, when working with full thrust close to deep loaded lighters as can be the case in narrow harbour basins.
IV. Furthermore, loss of pulling effectiveness due to the propeller wash impinging on the ship's hull is less compared to tugs with azimuth thruster which have a more concentrated propeller wash.
V. The full bow of tractor-tugs and the flat and wide hull bottoms which are necessary to create sufficient room for the propulsion units sometimes adversely affect their sea keeping behaviour. Questão 133

Conventional tugs have certain advantages and so do reverse-tractor tugs. ASD-tugs are nearly the same as reverse-tractor tugs but are designed in such a way that they can operate like a reverse-tractor tug as well as a conventional tug, thus combining the advantages of both types. ASD-tugs have a towing winch forward and a towing winch (can be optional) or towing hook aft. Some ASD-tugs have only one winch which is capable to operate over the bow as well as over the stern. When operating over the stern the towline runs via a trunk through the deckhouse to aft. This, for instance, is the case with TundRA 3000 ice class tugs for Svitzer and the ASD 2312 tug Jupiter of Boluda Towage Europe. The winches of these tugs are located in an enclosed area in the superstructure. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), the aft towing point is at a suitable location for towing on a line, namely: (Cap.2, p.37)

D) 0.35-0.4 x LWL from the stern.

E) 0.25-0.3 x LWL from the forward.

I. Excellent manoeuvrability, which includes among other things turning on the spot with a high rate of turn, astern speed equal to ahead speed and a side-stepping speed of approximately six knots.

IV. High side thrust up to 100 per cent of bollard pull to assist vessels through narrow passages, locks and bridges.
V. There is hardly any risk of damaging the azimuth thrusters on the bulb of an assisted ship as can happen with stern drive tugs, due to the thruster location and protection. Questão 142

According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), the basic principle of this tug is a radial system. With a radial system, a tug's heel due to a transverse towline force is limited. Performance and safety of several conventional tugs has thus been increased significantly. The higher the speed the higher the forces that can safely be generated in the towline and applied to the ship to be assisted. This tug is not specifically designed for tug operations at the ship's side as applied in many ports around the world. Basically, this tug design can most effectively be applied in situations where tug assistance is required during a transit, such as in channels, fairways and port approaches, more or less as an escort tug: (Cap.2, p.47-49)

A) Rotortug

B) Z-Tech tug

C) Carrousel tug

D) DOT tug

E) RSD tug

Questão 149

Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), typical for all these tugs is the in-line thruster arrangement, with one thruster at each end on the centreline of the tug, and the towing point in between the thrusters. The designer claims this layout offers superior effectiveness and competitiveness for ship-assist and escort tugs in the range of 22-40m. The standard propulsion system is a diesel-electric/diesel direct hybrid propulsion system, with batteries as option. Standard bridge equipment is two separate thruster controls. Joystick, or master-pilot system, is an option. High static and dynamic stability, high freeboard, well protected and in the centre line located thrusters (avoiding steel-steel contact with the assisted vessel) and easy tug handling. A subdivision of the hull in five compartments which are closed during operations (certified unmanned engine room) and double outside doors for the accommodation makes it virtually unsinkable. From a builder’s viewpoint, these tugs are developed for simple construction and require significantly less steel and only a minimum of equipment. Construction of these tugs is thus not only economical but also fast. The intrinsic simplicity of the concept and forward thinking during the development phase make these tugs fit for the installation of LNG or CNG tanks, exhaust-gas after-treatment systems, all-electric propulsion systems (including high-power podded drives and rim-driven thrusters) and further automation of propulsion control systems, such as a single-joystick control system: (Cap.2, p.56)

A) All-Rounder tug

B) SDM

C) EDDY

D) Giano tug

E) Z-Tech tug

Questão 154

When the tug's propeller wash is more or less with the direction of the water flow, the propeller is said to be operating in positive flow conditions. This is, for instance, when a bow tug is pulling a ship having headway. When the tug's propeller wash is more or less against the direction of the water flow, it is said to be operating in negative flow conditions. A) smaller - negative - increase - negative

B) smaller - negative - decrease - negative

C) greater - positive - decrease - positive

D) greater - negative - increase - negative

E) smaller - positive - increase - positive

Questão 157

Consider the following statements regarding to “Tug performance”: (Cap.2, p.78-79)
I. When the tug's propeller wash is more or less with the direction of the water flow, the propeller is said to be operating in positive flow conditions. This is, for instance, when a bow tug is pulling a ship having headway.
II. When the tug' s propeller wash is more or less against the direction of the water flow, it is said to be operating in negative flow conditions. This is, for instance, when a stern tug is braking a ship's speed.
III. The line pull is essentially dependent on the square of the propeller revolutions, and the engine power is dependent on the root of square of the revolutions.
IV. Tugs with the same BHP may have a different bollard pull depending on whether the propellers are fitted in a nozzle or not.
V. Propeller performance is also shown in so-called thrust vector diagrams. The achievable ahead thrust per 100bhp installed power as shown in the diagrams is 1.5 tons for a VS tug, 1.4 tons for tugs with azimuth thrusters and 1.1 tons for conventional tugs with propellers in nozzles. Omni-directional stern tugs are sometimes used to brake the ship’s speed or to keep the ship's speed at a low level of, eg, 3-4 knots which is lower than the ship's Dead Slow Ahead speed, while the ship keeps its engine running in order to be able to steer. Questão 180

Tugs operating alongside in the push-pull mode have the advantage that they can act rather quickly with short response times, which is of particular importance during berthing operations. The risks associated with tugs working in close proximity to the bow of a ship having speed, such as interaction forces and turning moments, are negated. On the other hand, there are some drawbacks having tugs operating in this way: (Cap.3, p.86-87)
I. Towlines are often very short which presents a problem in wave conditions; and, When pulling with short towlines, there is a loss of pulling effectiveness caused by the tug's propeller wash hitting the ship's hull at right angles. Tugs may lengthen the towline if equipped with a towing winch and so decrease the negative effect.
II. In case of conventional tugs, pulling effectiveness is low due to the low propeller performance when engines are running astern.
III. Combined width of ship and tugs can be a problem in narrow passages such as locks and bridges.
IV. Tugs must be used on the opposite side to which ship is to berth. There is no flexibility in berthing side as soon as the tugs are fastened alongside.
V. When the ship has to be turned, the turning lever is relatively short, namely, the distance between the two tugs at ship's side. VI. With high windage ships and tugs fastened at the leeside, the tugs may become jammed between ship and shore or quay if the winds or gusts become too strong. VII. The tug masters have only little insight into the manoeuvres being undertaken by the pilot. De acordo com o contido no livro “Tug use in Port, a Practical Guide” (Captain Henk Hensen - 4ª edição: 2021), analise as afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta:

A) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (III), (IV) e (VI) estão corretas

B) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II), (III) e (IV) estão corretas

C) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II), (III), (IV), (V) e (VII) estão corretas

D) Todas as afirmativas estão corretas

E) Apenas as afirmativas (II), (III) e (VII) estão corretas

Questão 181

There is a relationship between a type of tug and assisting method used. An essential factor is whether a tug should be suitable to operate at a ship's side, tow on a line, or both. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Relationship between type oftug and assisting method” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.3, p.87-88)

A) ln the ports of Japan, Taiwan and Hong Kong there is one assisting method and mainly one type of tug. The reverse-tractor tug with its omnidirectional propulsion at the stern is well suited to operate the assisting method in use - on a line at a ship's stern and alongside at the forward shoulder. ASD-tugs are, however, also used for this method. It is anticipated that for these ports the reverse-tractor tug is the type that will usually be ordered in the future.

B) ln Europe towing on a line is general practice, originally just with conventional tugs. Due to the limitations of conventional tugs, various tug types with omnidirectional propulsion are now increasingly being used, resulting in a change to more flexible assisting methods. This is the case in many other ports where originally mainly conventional tugs were used.

C) ln the USA tugs operate at a ship's side most of the time, and for many years the conventional tug was practically the only type to be found. The limited manoeuvrability and low astern power of these tugs is partly compensated for by the use of extra towlines, installation of high engine power, specific propeller / rudder configurations and/ or specific assisting methods.

D) In many ports of the USA and Canada there is a tendency towards the use of more flexible tug types - tractor tugs as well as reverse-tractor or ASD-tugs. As in many ports elsewhere, conventional tugs will not continue to be built in the future.

E) There is often a steady development towards a particular tug type. For instance, years ago there were still several VS tugs in the Port of Yokohama. This type has now been replaced by the typical Japanese type of tug, the reverse-tractor type.

Questão 182

Navy ships do visit commercial ports regularly. In navy ports they know how to handle their ships and they often have purpose-designed tugs with well-trained tug masters. This is not the case in commercial ports. As the frequency of visits is low, the level of knowledge and experience to handle these ships is equally low while tugs are not specifically designed for handling all types of navy ships. Several of the navy ship types can be handled in the same way as merchant navy ships. Although, information about allowable maximum hull force is important when tugs have to push. However, there are two types of navy ships that require specific attention, viz. submarines and aircraft carriers. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Submarines” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.3, p.95-96)

A) Although there are twin screw submarines, most of them are single screw. They have generally good steerage at about two knots and more. Submarine turning circles are naturally small.

B) Many submarines can tow a sonar (line-array) and have therefore equipment near the stern rudder (a tow-box); depending on the submarine an anchor light may also be placed there. Other submarines may have the anchor light on top of the rudder.

C) Care should be taken by tugs that the tow-box and/or anchor light are not damaged by a towline or a tug. This can easily happen when a towline under tension must be replaced from port to starboard and vice versa. However, such a tug manoeuvre is only possible with submarines that have the planes and rudder in an X-configuration that only just appears above the waterline. With all other submarines the rudder is in the way.

D) Tiles are an important aspect for tug masters to be aware of when handling submarines. Tiles on the hull of a submarine absorb the sound waves of active sonar from another vessel, reducing and distorting the return signal, thereby reducing its effective range. Furthermore, they reduce the sounds emitted from the submarine, particularly its engines, and reduce the range at which it can be detected by passive sonar (a passive sonar listens).

E) Merchant navy ships have indicated on the hull where tugs can push. On submarine it is the opposite way. There are No Pushing/’No Push’ areas where sensitive equipment is located.

Questão 183

In navy ports tugs are often designed for the work to be carried out. In most cases these are highly manoeuvrable tugs with good fendering. The profile of such tugs together with the underwater fendering these tugs have, fits well with the profile of the submarines to be assisted whether alongside or when pushing. Tugs in commercial ports do not have these features. How tug assistance is carried out in the various comercial ports depends on the local situation, environmental conditions, and available tugs and tug types. As tugs in commercial ports are not fitted out as tugs in well equipped navy ports, the way tug assistance to be carried out should be such that submarines will not be damaged by the assisting tugs. This means that towing on a line, with tugs fastened fore and aft via the bull ring (a kind of panama fairleads) on the bollards of the submarine, will be the best, this in combination with the push-pull method. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), in this case the best suited tug type is the: (Cap.3, p.96-98)

A) combi-tugs

B) VS tractor tugs

C) ASD-tugs

D) tractor tugs

E) conventional tugs

Questão 184

In some ports, tugs might not be needed, where aircraft carriers can safely anchor within the harbour limits, although tugs will then be stand by for in case they are needed. It is of importance that all involved in the handling of the aircraft carrier are informed carefully about the tug locations, procedures to be followed and manoeuvres to be carried out. So it is vital that information about the various tasks is passed to pilots and tug masters well in advance. The pilot should also hold a pilot/tug master exchange with the tug masters when he has boarded the submarine in order to confirm the intentions. How aircraft carriers are handled by tugs in comercial ports depends, as with submarines, again on the local situation, fairway width and alignment, environmental conditions and available tugs and tug types. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Aircraft carriers” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.3, p.99)

A) The recommended tug types are Voith tugs and tugs with azimuth propulsion and all with proper fendering.

B) If the aircraft carrier has to berth, four tugs are generally recommended for commercial ports. Depending on tug, the way tug assistance is carried out, and aircraft carrier design, additional fenders between tug and ship might be needed.

C) When tugs have to pull to keep the ship up into the wind, a large loss in pulling effectiveness can occur when the tug propeller wash is hitting the ship’s hull near right angles. The larger the distance between ship and tug the higher the loss. This factor should be taken into account for the total required bollard if such as situation may happen.

D) There is one basic requirement for tugs handling aircraft carriers and that is that due to the large overhanging parts of aircraft carriers, tugs should be able to lower their mast, especially in ports where tugs are operating alongside, although it depends again on aircraft carrier design.

E) In general aircraft carriers have sufficient bollards and fairleads on the mooring deck and sunken bollards in the sites to secure towing lines. It is important for towing companies and tug masters to know the SWL of the bollards as well as the maximum allowable hull pressure. If the aircraft carrier would anchor within the port limits two suitable and powerful escorting tugs are recommended.

Questão 189

In the figure a tug is pushing forward. Although the pivot point lies further aft, the effect forward is low because of the opposing hydrodynamic forces also centred forward. When starboard rudder is also applied the pivot point moves further forward. A force applied to a ship, for instance a tug force or a rudder force, gives a transverse force and a turning moment, resulting in a lateral velocity and a rate of turn. The arrow V is the direction ship’s centre of gravity (g) may move as a result of the lateral velocity caused by the rudder force or tug force, and the forward velocity of the ship. The lateral movement of the ship is opposed by the hydrodynamic forces centred forward on the ship having headway, which also creates a turning moment. This turning moment turning opposes the moments created by the tugs. The location of the pivot point (PP) results from the motion of the ship caused by the various forces working on the ship. If in situation of figure the tug would push a bit more aft, it may push behind the forward lying centre of hydrodynamic forces. The result would be that the ship will turn to port instead of turning to stardboard. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), this is called the: (Cap.4, p.101)

A) Bernoulli Effect

B) Transverse Effect

C) Coanda Effect

D) Donkey Effect

E) Shallow water Effect

B) in a position approximately two third of the ship's length from the bow.

D) somewhere between stern and amidships.

E) in a position approximately one third of the ship's length from the bow.

Questão 191

(PROVA PSPP 2008) Analise as afirmativas abaixo: (Cap.4, p.101) I) quando um navio está parado e o propulsor aplica um “thrust” avante, com o leme carregado para um dos bordos, o ponto de pivô é posicionado entre a proa e meia-nau. II) um navio se deslocando, com um rebocador empurrando para um dos bordos pela proa, o ponto de pivô fica entre a popa e meia-nau. Questão 196

The Figure below shows a tug moving ahead, towing on a line, assisting a ship under speed: (Cap.4, p.103) The resultant force created by incoming water flow is force F, assumed to be centred approximately near amidships, location C. Force F can be resolved into lift force L and drag force D, comparable with the lift and drag forces on rudders or aeroplane wings. Lift force L gives an additional force on the towline and drag force D has to be overcome by the tug's thrust. Towing point T lies a little behind the centre of pressure C. The force in the towline in combination with force L creates a counter-clockwise turning moment. Consider two locations of propulsion - position Ps for stern driven tugs, a conventional tug for example, and position Pt for tractor tugs. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements and choose the incorrect alternative:

A) The smaller the distance between T and C the smaller is the turning moment. Thus less steering power, by either rudder deflection or omnidirectional propellers, is needed to counteract that turning moment. Consequently, more engine Power is available for towing.

B) Towline forces also create list. Considering the direction of steering forces it is evident that with the propulsion located in position Ps the sideways steering forces increase the fug's list, while with propulsion located in Pt steering forces counteract the list caused by the towline force.

C) With increasing speed, force F increases and consequently lift force L. The higher the speed the more steering effort is needed. Therefore, the higher the speed the larger the difference in towline forces between a conventional and tractor tug.

D) If propulsion is located aft at Ps, starboard rudder or thrust is needed, giving a little more drag but also an additional force in the towline.

E) If propulsion is located forward (Pt) then sideways steering power is needed, but in the opposite direction. This consequently increases the towline force.

The direct and indirect towing methods are explained in figure: P is the propulsion, C is the location of the centre of pressure and T is the towing point: (Cap.4, p.105) Top: Direct Towing Method - A: Tractor tug; B: ASD/Reverse-tractor tug Position 1: Steering and retarding Position 2: Retarding. Bottom: lndirect Towing Method - A: Tractor tug B: ASD/Reverse-tractor tug. Position 1: Steering and retarding Position 2: Retarding. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements and choose the incorrect alternative:

A) The indirect towing method is applied by an after tug at speeds higher than five to six knots. With the indirect towing method, the tug makes use of the hydrodynamic forces created by incoming water flow on the tug's skeg and/ or underwater body. The aft lying towing point of the tractor tug, and consequently the small distance between towing point (T) and centre of pressure (C), implies that only a little crosswise steering power of a tug is needed to keep the tug in the most effective position to exert the highest steering forces to the assisted ship.

B) The direct towing method is carried out by an after tug on a line at relatively low ship speeds. The tug pulls in the required direction, either to give steering assistance and/ or to control the ship's speed. Tractor tugs assist with their bow directed towards the stern of the assisted ship and ASD / reverse-tractor types of tug assist with their stern towards the stern.

C) Whether tractor tugs or ASD/reverse-tractor tugs are more effective in steering control depends on the relation between the distance P-T and C-T, the tug's engine power and thrust performance in the pulling direction, but also on the tug's underwater plane. The smaller the distance CT in relation to PT the better the tug's performance in the direct towing mode.

D) The ASD-tug/reverse-tractor tug has generally a larger distance between the towing point (T) and centre of pressure (C). Consequently, more crosswise power is needed to keep the tug in the most effective position, thus decreasing towline force.

E) A more forward lying towing point in a tractor tug, closer to (C), results in higher towline forces, but the safety of operations in the indirect mode and as a result performance decreases. A more forward lying centre of pressure in ASD / reverse-tractor tugs does not affect tug safety but increases the tug's performance as a stern tug.

Questão 202

There is a large variety of skegs and ongoing research for the best skeg type. Mainly the following skegs can be found on tugs: (Cap.4, p.106)
I. The skeg on tractor tugs provides better course stability when free-sailing ahead (with skeg aft). It generates additional towing forces when operatirig as stern tug in the indirect towing mode because it increases the tug's lateral underwater area and brings the centre of pressure more aft, closer to the towing point. The skeg may have a specific form to generate the highest possible lift forces.
II. An aft skeg on tugs not being tractor tugs: A vertical fin attached to the tug's underwater hull in the centreline of the after section at some distance before the propellers, to give the tug a better course stability when free-sailing ahead.
III. A flat vertical skeg, or box keel, in the centreline of several ASD-tugs and reverse-tractor tugs, which extends for some distance before the propellers to the forefoot and so bringing the centre of pressure more forward, closer to the forward towing point. It provides also better course stability when free-sailing ahead and often, depending on skeg form, particularly astern, because this is crucial for safe bow-to-bow operations with a ship having headway. The skeg generates additional towing forces when operating as stern tug in the indirect towing mode and when ASD-tugs operate as conventional tugs ata ship having speed.
IV. Skeg at the bow of an ASD or reverse-tractor tug. Such a skeg improves the course stability when free- sailing astern (not ahead) and increases a tug's performance when operating as stern tug in the indirect mode and to some extent as bow tug when operating bow-to-bow at a ship having headway.
V. Twin skegs on an ASD-tug increase course stability when sailing astern, decrease the turning moment due to the relatively larger distance between the thrusters and the more forward lying point of application of the hydrodynamic forces; the latter also increases performance in the indirect operation mode and as bow tug when operating bow-to-bow; the application of twin skegs reduces interaction between thrusters and skegs. VI. Twin skegs as can be seen on some azimuth tractor tugs and double skegs as is the case with the Giano tug. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

A) (II) and (V) solely are true

B) (I), (III) and (VI) solely are true

C) All of them are true

D) (I), (II), (IV), (V) and (VI) solely are true

E) (I), (II), (III), (IV) and (VI) solely are true

Questão 214

Tugs operating at a ship's side need good astern power, which should be about the same as their ahead power. Tugs with omnidirectional propulsion are therefore very suitable for push-pull work. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Tugs operating at a ship’s side – Pulling mode” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.4, p.118-119)

A) The longer tugs can pull effectively with increasing ship speed the better. lt is obvious that the conventional tug will swing around. The tug needs a stern line leading forward to be able to pull at right angles. For this situation, the paddle-wheel effect of the tug's propeller also adds to the swinging motion. Tugs with twin screws, steering nozzles, a Towmaster system or flanking rudders perform better.

B) An important aspect to take into account is loss of pulling efficiency due to a tug's propeller wash hitting a ship's hull. This force can be as large as its bollard pull, sometimes even larger. The effect is far less if the distance between tug propeller and ship's hull is decreased.

C) When changing from pulling to pushing tug captains should be aware of the dynamic forces in a towline. Particularly with a steep towline angle and in wave conditions these forces may draw the tug quickly in the direction of the ship when its engine is suddenly stopped. When stern thrust is also applied a tug may hit a ship's hull with force.

D) Tractor and ASD/reverse-tractor tugs perform much better, because while pulling they can apply forces in the direction of ship's movement. That is a big advantage of omnidirectional propulsion systems engaged in push-pull operations.

E) Tractor tugs therefore push and pull with their stern so as to keep their propellers as far away as possible from a ship's hull. In addition, tractor tugs with azimuth propellers, when pulling, can set their propeller thrusters at an angle, thus diverting the propeller wash. The same applies to ASD/reverse-tractor tugs. Higher pulling effectiveness can also be achieved using a longer towline.

Questão 218

In figure below different tug positions are given. A ship has headway and has to make a turn to starboard. Tugs have to assist. Attention now turns to the effect on a ship when tugs are operating in one of these positions. The location of the pivot point is taken into account. (Cap.4, p.124-125) According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements and choose the incorrect alternative:

A) Tug nº 5 is in a very effective position. The longest possible lever for steering forces and the transverse forces centred forward contribute to the swing. Also, the tug does not increase ship's speed. Tug nº 6 is in a similarly effective position to tug nº 5, but has the disadvantage that this tug increases ship’s speed.

B) Tug nº 3 can assist the starboard turn by going astern. The tug’s underwater resistance contributes to the starboard swing. If tug nº 2 had a bow line, both tugs 2 and 3 are in good position to take off ship’s headway, if required.

C) Position of tug nº 2 is not so good for steering assistance required. The tug has to overcome the same transverse forces as tug nº 1, but the lever of crosswise steering forces exerted by the tug is much shorter and the tug’s underwater resistance opposes the turn. Also, when a tug is unable to push at right angles to a ship’s hull it will decrease a ship’s speed.

D) Tug nº 4 is in an effective position to assist the starboard turn by pushing, because of the long lever and forward centred lateral resistance, which contributes to the swing. When tug nº 4 cannot work at right angles, ship's speed increases, but as a result of the higher rate of turn caused by the pushing tug and consequently the higher drift angle, ship's speed is hardly affected.

E) Forward tug nº 1, towing on a line, is capable of exerting quite high crosswise steering forces on a ship. The effect can be limited because of the transverse forces near a ship’s bow to be overcome. It is clear that for a particular ship these transverse forces are proportional to the draft and underkeel clearance. Also, the more the tug is pulling in line with a ship’s heading the more the tug will increase a ship’s speed.

Questão 222

In wave conditions harbour tugs can, instead of towing on a line, operate more effectively and at higher wave heights at the ship's leeside, if circumstances and ship manoeuvres allow. It all depends on the local situation. In wave conditions the risk of girting for conventional tugs towing on a line is higher than in calm water. Passing towlines can be carried out more safely with more highly manoeuvrable tugs. Tractor tugs will in particular operate more safely and can provide assistance in somewhat larger wave heights. Anyway, waves limit the operating effectiveness of harbour tugs when towing on a line as well as operating at a ship's side when exposed to waves. Performance decreases with increasing wave height. Questão 223

At the approach of several ports, apart from wind driven waves, swell can be a critical factor for fastening tugs as can also be the case at offshore terminals. As with waves, in case of swell it is not only the wave height but also the relative wave direction with respect to ship’s heading and the wave period. For swell it is assumed here wave periods of 8 secs and higher and for tug assistance modern high manoeuvrable tugs, such as Voith tugs and ASD- or ATD-tugs. In case of swell the following is needed: (CAp.4, p.128)
I. long heaving lines as tugs cannot approach close to the ship.
II. long light pennants or grommets made of HMPE fibres.
III. a messenger long and strong enough to be able to get successfully up to the ship, through fairleads around rollers and several turns around the winch warping head or capstan.
IV. Also, towlines should be long enough to avoid large peak loads in the line because of the difference in movements between tug and ship due to swell, for instance an 80 – 100m long towline. Questão 225

Depending on the local situation, tug assistance on arrival or departure generally comprises three phases: (Cap.5, p.135)
I. The phase whereby a ship has reasonable speed: The ship can still use her engines and rudder to compensate for drift forces caused by wind, current and/ or waves, by steering a drift angle. Depending on the situation, tugs may assist.
II. The intermediate phase: When a ship has to reduce speed, entering a dock, harbour basin, tuming circle or approaching a berth. The ship also has to be stopped within a certain distance. When reducing speed, a ship's steering performance also decreases. The propeller has to be stopped, the influence of wind and current increases and tug assistance is needed more frequently and to a larger extent.
III. The phase involving the final part of the arrival manoeuvre: The ship is practically dead in the water, such as in the turning circle and/ or when berthing. The ship is very restricted in manoeuvring performance and not able to compensate for wind and current forces. Tugs have to assist fully. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), for ships influenced by wind, current and waves, when a ship is stopped in the water, which phase is most important for assessment of the bollard pull required?

A) Phase II

B) Phase I

C) Phase I and phase III

D) Phase III

E) Phase II and phase III

Questão 226

The following main factors influence tug assistance: (Cap.5, p.135)
I. Port particulars, including: restrictions of fairways, port entrance, passage to a berth, turning circle, manoeuvring space at a berth or harbour basin, available stopping distance, locks, bridges, moored vessels, water depths, speed restrictions and so on.
II. Berth construction, including: type of berth (open, e.g. jetty or solid).
III. The ship, including: type size, draft and underkeel clearance, trim, windage, engine power ahead/astern, propeller type, manoeuvring performance, and availability of side thrusters and specific rudders.
IV. Environmental conditions, including: wind, current, waves, visibility, ice.
V. Method of assistance, including: Towing on a line, operating at a ship’s side or a combination. De acordo com o contido no livro “Tug use in Port, a Practical Guide” (Captain Henk Hensen - 4ª edição: 2021), analise as afirmativas aos fatores que influenciam no cálculo do bollard pull necessário, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta:

Questão 229

According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), the total force acting on a ship could, in theory, be compensated for by tugs when bollard pull equals the total forces of wind, curtent and waves. However, there are some important factors to be taken into account: (Cap.5, p.136) ( ) Tugs are not always pulling or pushing at right angles to a ship. Fot instance, during arrival or departure manoeuvres, a ship may have some forward or astern movement. Tugs try to keep pace with a ship, and thus use engine power in the direction of ship's movement at the expense of pull or push forces. The same happens in situations where there is a current and a ship has relative speed through the water. ( ) Bollard pull actually available may, due to wear and fouling, no longer be a full 100% compared to the original bollard pull tests. ( ) Forward and after tugs often cannot pull or push at full power simultaneously, even when the required bollard pull forward and aft is carefully considered, taking into account possible yaw moments caused by wind and/ or current or trim. A ship may start to swing. At one end of the ship the tug then has to reduce power in order to stop the swing. ( ) The propeller wash of tugs towing on a line may hit a ship's hull and increase pulling effectiveness. This can be influenced to a certain extent by correct towline length and towing angle. ( ) Tugs must have sufficient reserve power to push or pull a ship up against wind and current or to stop a drifting ship quickly enough and bring it back to the required track. ( ) When calculating the forces of wind, current and waves on a ship, a specified safety factor should be taken into account for BP required. In the graphs showing BP required to keep a ship up against a beam wind, cross current and beam waves, a safety factor of 20% is included. For tugs pulling at a ship's side this safety factor is also sufficient.

A) (T) (T) (F) (T) (F) (T)

B) (F) (F) (F) (T) (F) (T)

C) (F) (T) (T) (T) (F) (T)

D) (F) (F) (T) (F) (T) (F)

E) (T) (T) (T) (F) (T) (F)

Questão 237

Consider the following statements regarding to “Wind forces”: (Cap.5, p.138)
I. Wind velocity also varies by height. For calculating wind force in the equations, basically its velocity at the standard 10 metres height should be used. For wind velocities obtained at a different elevation, adjustments to the equivalent 10 metre velocity can be made with this formula VW = vW . (10/h)½.
II. For certain ships the standard wind height may be too high, while for some other ships it might even be too low, as can be the case with large fully loaded container ships, car carriers, LNG carriers, etc.
III. The drift speed of a ship decreases with underkeel clearance, because the forces created by the opposing water decrease when underkeel clearance gets smaller.
IV. A smaller drift speed does not imply that less bollard pull is needed. A drifting vessel has to be stopped and pulled back through the water. Stopping a ship from drifting and pulling back also needs more power in shallow water than in deep water.
V. The amount of water moving with a ship when drifting, the added mass, also increases with decreasing underkeel clearance, requiring additional bollard pull to stop and pull back a drifting vessel in shallow water. Questão 245

However, following corrections are recommended for tug forces needed to control cross current forces in case of small underkeel clearances: (Cap.5, p.139)

A) LNG carriers: no corrections Ballasted tanker: water depth to draft ratio 1.05 - 10% less tug force. Loaded tanker: water depth to draft ratio 1.05 - 20% less tug force. Loaded tanker: water depth to drat ratio 1.10 - 30% less tug force.

B) Ballasted tanker: no corrections Loaded tanker: water depth to draft ratio 1.05 - 5% less tug force. Loaded tanker: water depth to draft ratio 1.10 - 10% less tug force. LNG carriers: water depth to drat ratio 1.10 - 30% less tug force.

E) Ballasted tanker: no corrections Loaded tanker: water depth to draft ratio 1.05 - 5% less tug force. Loaded tanker: water depth to draft ratio 1.10 - 10% less tug force. LNG carriers: water depth to drat ratio 1.10 - 10% less tug force.

Questão 246

Depending on environmental conditions in and around a port, wave forces may also be a factor to be considered when establishing the bollard pull required. Harbour tugs can only operate effectively up to a certain maximum wave height, so only short beam seas are considered. It is difficult to calculate wave forces exactly. It is assumed that a ship’s draft is large enough to reflect the waves completely. Because of the relatively short wave period it is further assumed that waves do not cause any ship motion. In practical terms it means we are considering conditions such as those found in windy but sheltered areas. The waves are short and steep and the wave length is small relative to the length of the ship. We are not considering open areas, where ocean waves or swell might impinge upon the ship and cause it to heave, roll and pitch. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), a 25 per cent safety margin is again added, and converting to kilograms instead of Newtons and wave amplitude in significant wave height, the simplified formula for roughly calculating the bollard pull required to hold a ship up against short period beam waves reads: (Cap.5, p.140)

A) Fwave = 110 V2 LBP T (kgf)

B) Fwave = 150 L HS2 (kgf)

C) Fwave = 112 L HS2 (kgf)

D) Fwave = 40 V2 LBP T (kgf)

E) Fwave = 185 V2 LBP T (kgf)

Questão 247

Dependendo das condições ambientais dentro e ao redor de um porto, as forças das ondas também podem ser um fator a ser considerado ao estabelecer a força de tração necessária. Os rebocadores portuários só podem operar eficazmente até uma determinada altura máxima de onda e, por isso, são considerados apenas mares pequenos de través. É difícil calcular com exatidão as forças das ondas. Supõe-se que o calado de um navio seja grande o suficiente para refletir completamente as ondas e, devido ao período relativamente curto das ondas, presume-se ainda que as ondas não causam qualquer movimento do navio. Em termos práticos, significa que são considerados condições como as encontradas em áreas com muito vento, porém abrigadas. As ondas são curtas e íngremes e o comprimento das ondas é pequeno em relação ao comprimento do navio. De acordo com o contido no livro “Tug use in Port, a Practical Guide” (Captain Henk Hensen - 4ª edição: 2021), considerando que um navio tem comprimento entre perpendiculares de 200 m e altura estimada de onda é de 1 m, podemos afirmar que a força das ondas de través sobre o navio é então, aproximadamente: (Cap.5, p.140)

A) Fwave = 16,5 tons

B) Fwave = 22 tons

D) Fwave = 28 tons

Questão 251

The larger a ship's displacement the more bollard pull is needed to stop sideways movement. Not only the displacement but also the water mass moving with a ship influences bollard pull required. This is called 'added' or 'hydrodynamic' mass. Virtual mass is the sum of displacement and added mass. The exact amount of added mass is difficult to determine. The added mass increases with decreasing underkeel clearance. Berth construction also affects approach speed. Solid berths reduce a ship's approach speed because a water cushion builds up between ship and berth. Open berths or jetties do not reduce approach speed as the water can flow away in any direction. Questão 255

Loaded tankers and bulk carriers with large displacement need the largest tug power for controlling transverse speed. These ships are less affected by wind. When there is any current, berth construction should be such that the current runs in line with the berth or jetty, though unfortunately this is not always so. In any case, tugs should have sufficient reserve power to compensate for any current and/or wind effect. In general, when handling heavy ships, tugs use a substantial part of their power to control transverse approach speed towards a berth. As draft decreases the bollard pull required for controlling transverse speed becomes less. Lateral wind area increases and consequently available bollard pull can be used to keep the ship up into wind, current and/or waves, if necessary. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), newer tugs are able to operate for a limited time at: (Cap.5, p.141)

E) 100 per cent MCR

Questão 271

Following Bernoulli's theory there are reduced pressure areas round a ship where the relative velocity of water flow is increased. If stream lines were parallel around a ship the reduction in pressure would be uniform. Well ahead of a ship the stream lines are equally spaced, but at a certain point they are wedged apart and as they go round the body of the ship they are compressed. At the stern the stream lines tend to spread again in an effort to fill the gap astern of the ship. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “lnteraction effects influencing tug safety” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.6, p.151)

A) When stream lines converge, water speed increases and water pressure reduces. This boils down to conservation of energy in fluid flow. At low speeds a ship's wave making resistance is minimal. The wave pattern generated by a ship travelling at higher speeds causes wave making and wave braking (at the bow) resistance.

B) Pressure fields caused by the Bernoulli effect are the main cause of the wave pattern around a ship at low speeds. It means that at the bow there is a high pressure area, a bow wave, followed by a low pressure field around the midsection while at the stern there is again a high pressure area, although lower than that at the bow.

C) When stream lines diverge the speed of the water reduces and, according to Bernoulli, pressure increases.

D) Due to viscous resistance or skin friction water is dragged along with a ship, a little at the bow but more and more towards the stern. It forms a fairly dead layer of water, called the boundary layer, increasing in thickness from bow to stern. Abaft the stern the boundary layer forms the frictional wake. This boundary layer and wake astern of a ship result in a less marked spreading of stream lines, resulting in a greater high pressure field near the stern than at the bow.

E) Particularly in the case of wide bodied ships, water speeds up round the forward but less round the aft shoulders, causing a local wave trough.

Questão 272

In figure, a tug is slowly overtaking a bulk Carrier and travelling past the ship. The most relevant interaction effects on the tug are now considered. The approximate stream lines around the ship are shown. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Tug-ship interaction with respect to tug safety” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.6, p.152) Note: The tug shown in figure is an ASD-tug, but could also be a conventional tug. When referring below to port or starboard rudder this can also be interpreted as comparable thruster settings.

A) When moving a little further forward (between positions 4 and 5) the outward turning moment suddenly changes into an inward turning moment. This is due to the cross flow near the bow of the ship acting on the tug's rudder/thrusters or skeg as a steering force. Due to the lift force caused by the cross flow the tug drifts sideways away from the ship.

B) As soon as the tug moves further forward and parallel with the ship's hull it experiences a sudden outward turning moment, caused by the tug's bow cushion. In addition, the tug's stern is near the wave trough at the aft quarter (position 2) where the water speed between the tug's stern and ship's hull is high. As a consequence the stern is sucked towards the ship. The tug is also sucked bodily towards the ship.

C) When coming nearly abeam of the stern (position 1) the tug is sucked towards the ship because the speed of water increases between tug and ship's hull causing a low pressure field and consequently a suction force towards the ship. Since the tug's forepart is closer to the ship than the stern the tug experiences a starboard turning moment.

D) As it proceeds the tug's bow reaches the trough near the aft quarter of the ship, causing a decreased turning effect to starboard and the tug needs more power in order to maintain speed due to the higher water speed encountered.

E) When the tug approaches the stern from a position behind tug n° 1, it experiences an increase of speed due to the relatively low water speed. The tug may be pushed sideways to starboard as well by the incoming waterflow.
