I. A tug's underwater body and appendages have their influences as well, especially on the turning moments.
II. Although interaction effects differ by ship and tug, these do exist and one should be aware of them. The smaller the distance between tug and ship the larger interaction effects are. Shallow water and narrow waters have an increasing effect on interaction between tug and ship.
III. Most important to keep in mind is that the influence of all interaction effects increases sharply with speed and are most dangerous near a ship's stern.
IV. Ship speeds can be rather high when tugs are coming alongside or making fast. Speeds up to 5 knots are quite normal for tugs taking or passing a towline near a ship's bow or stern. Higher speeds are not uncommon, even up to 9 or 10 knots. The interaction effects are then large, especially for tugs taking a line at the bow. With such high speeds highly manoeuvrable tugs with a high, free sailing speed are required and, of course, very experienced tug captains. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I), (II) and (III) solely are true

B) (I) and (IV) solely are true

C) (II), (III) and (IV) solely are true

D) All of them are true

E) (I), (II) and (IV) solely are true

To make it even more difficult, with changes in tug position the situation may change rapidly. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Tug-ship interaction with respect to tug performance” and mark true (T) or false (F): (Cap.6, p.154) ( ) The relative speed of water along a ship's hull between bow and stern increases in speed compared to a free stream. With wide body ships the water speed near the forward and aft shoulders might be even less than at the ship's midsection. ( ) A tug pushing at a ship's side is affected by this increased water speed and tug performance is adversely affected, particularly when operating at the ship's midsection (positions 1 and 2). Shallow and narrow waters increase water flow speed along the ship sides, further decreasing a tug's effectiveness. ( ) with respect to tug nº 3 the shorter the towline and the closer to the ship's hull, the larger the interaction effects are. It is advisable for tugs towing on a line, like tugs nº 3 and 4, to use a somewhat longer towline length and operate at a farther distance from the ship's hull, which is also safer. This reduces interaction effects and the negative effect of the tug's propeller wash impinging on the ship's hull. ( ) When retarding forces are required, a ship's propeller is normally stopped or astern thrust applied. Compared to a free stream situation the wake causes a decrease in the tug's underwater resistance and propeller braking performance, assuming the same amount of engine power is used, resulting in a smaller towline force. ( ) The wake is a combined influence of potential wake, frictional wake and wave pattern. ( ) As relative water speed in the ship's wake further decreases in shallow and narrow waters, the negative effect of the wake on a tug's braking performance increases. The effect of the propeller slipstream is the opposite. ( ) The most marked influence is experienced by tugs pushing at a ship's side and tugs applying braking forces in a ship's wake.

Questão 280

When considering tug-ship interaction it is safest, when coming alongside a ship underway at speed, to approach near the midsection where a more uniform flow pattern exists. At positions further forward or aft the interaction effects are larger and less predictable. Tug captains note from experience that when they apply astern thrust with the inner propeller, completely against the expected manoeuvring procedure, the tug comes free from the ship's hull. The explanation is that the water speed between ship and tug hulls decreases and consequently pressure rises. The increased water pressure between the two hulls, in combination with bow cushion effect, force the tug to come off. The higher a ship's speed the larger the suction forces. According to Capt. HenkHensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port”, which answer best fill in the blanks? (Cap.6, p.155) Tugs with azimuth propellers have the thrusters pointed somewhat _____ when proceeding at low speeds. A) inwards - inward - an increase

B) outwards - inward - a decrease

C) inwards - outward - a decrease

D) outwards - outward - an increase

E) outwards - inward - an increase

Questão 282

Consider the following statements: (Cap.6, p.156)
I. Taking or passing a towline at the bow of large loaded wide bodied ships is not so dangerous. Tug captains learn from experience that when near the fore part of the bow and steering a little inwards towards the bow, the tug does not get closer. However, when the tug is moving further forward it experiences turning moment towards the ship. This effect will always be largest with a small underkeel clearance.
II. A conventional tug approaches a ship under speed to take a towline at the bow. At a particular moment the tug captain considers his tug too close to the ship's hull and tries to clear the ship's side using engines full ahead while steering to port. Due to this action the tug is pushed against the ship by the steering forces and moves steadily forward along the ship's bow, unsuccessfully trying to get free. Finally the tug comes broadside under the bow and is run down. The only satisfactory manoeuvre in such circumstances is to go full astern.
III. A tractor type of tug is safer in such a situation, because the steering forces are directed away from the ship. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

A) (I) and (III) solely are true

B) (I) and (II) solely are true

C) None of them are true

D) All of them are true

E) (II) and (III) solely are true

Questão 283

Consider the following statements regarding to “Overtaking a bow tug on a line – Girting – Tripping”: (Cap.6, p.161)
I. The above situation is less dangerous for a tractor tug, and other tugs with the towing staple or towline fairlead at the end of the tug. Such tugs swing around on the towline and come alongside an attended ship unless the towline is released in time - so-called “tripping” (figure C).
II. Similar situations can arise with a tractor tug, and other tugs with the towing point at one end of the tug, when the towing angle - the angle between ship's heading and direction of the towline - is getting too large with respect to the forward speed of a ship. The tug is unable to come back in line with the ship and swings around.
III. The danger of 'girting' or 'tripping' does not only exist when a ship rounds a bend. Even when a ship is proceeding on a straight course girting can occur. In that case excessive speed of the ship is the main cause.
IV. When a ship increases speed to a level which is rather high for a forward tug towing on a line, the tug master probably does not keep position right ahead of a ship's bow, because that is too dangerous. The tug steers out towards a position more aside in order to keep well clear of the ship's bow. It is understandable that if ship's speed further increases, a comparable girting or tripping situation will arise for the tug.
V. Although pilots should be aware of the implications of too high a ship's speed for the safety of assisting tugs, it is again an indication of the importance of good communications between tug masters and pilots. The pilot may not have a good view of what is happening at the bow and the tug master should therefore inform the pilot in good time if he considers a speed increase too high. Questão 286

Dense fog creates a rather difficult situation for ships as well as for the attending tugs. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Low visibility” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.6, p.163-164)

A) In restricted visibility conditions, the ECDIS operator may then lose cues about safe distances to other objects within range and out of range of the screen by just staring at the ECDIS monitor. Zooming in too much may result in loss of distance refetences. In such conditions a second navigator should assist by actively using the distance measuring tool of the ECDIS.

B) It should be noted that the use of towing bitts is recommended for tugs towing on a line during fog conditions. However, in case of emergency it may be almost impossible and certainly dangerous to release the towline under tension rapidly.

C) Navigating on ECDIS should be done with great caution. When navigating on ECDIS in confined waters, the user tends to zoom the scale of the ENC (Electronic Navigational Chart) to very large when it comes to precise manoeuvring.

D) A PPU (Portable Pilot Unit) might be of help during fog. PPUs provide useful information tailored to the pilots' needs. However PPUs which obtain the main navigational parameters by means of an AIS pilot plug should not be used for navigation.

E) A problem for a pilot is also how to interpret the radar information towards correct rudder and engine orders. An example: During clear whether some pilots try to navigate on the radar as in fog in order to build up some more experience. What they then often see is that they initiate a turn later, and even sometimes too late, than they would do with clear visibility.

Questão 287

For a port, which should include harbour office, traffic control, pilot organisation, towing companies and boatmen, it is needed to have certain procedures for how to handle ships in case of fog, for instance when visibility is 500m or less. These procedures may indude such items as: (Cap.6, p.165)
I. For all involved: Consultations between port coordination centre, pilots, towing company and boatmen whether a ship can safely be handled and be allowed to enter or leave the port.
II. For the pilot: Information about visibility and visibility prospects for all parts of the fairway to be available; Whether current or tidal restrictions are needed; Safe speeds; and Fastening of tugs there where engines can be stopped during securing tugs and course changes are not needed.
III. For the port: Consultations between pilot and tug masters about which tugs best can be used and about best tug placement; and Information to be available whether berths are free and container cranes topped and/ or moved from the berth location.
IV. For the Traffic Control Centre: Care to be taken that sufficient distance between incoming and departing ships is arranged and concentrations of ships in the fairway towards and from the berth are avoided. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

D) All of them are true

Questão 290

Releasing tugs should take place when it can be expected that no further tug assistance is needed, and not earlier - which can sometimes be a critical decision. It can happen that with a departing ship, after tugs have been released in a fairway, an engine or rudder failure occurs. Tug assistance is then needed again. This takes time and a grounding might become inevitable. Although such a situation cannot be foreseen, it might be good to realise at the time and place where the tugs will be released that it might happen. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Releasing tugs” and mark true (T) or false (F): (Cap.6, p.172-173) ( ) If a crew on board ship is not able to release a tug's towline when required, problems may arise if a ship is already increasing speed. This is particularly the case with manilla towlines on powerful tugs. ( ) The towline has to be slacked off by a tug in order to make it possible for a ship's crew to release it. The slack towline is then dragged through the water. When ship's speed increases the resistance of the towline also increases, creating more tension in the line. Releasing it then becomes almost impossible. Chain stoppers, when used, may break. It is a difficult situation which can only be avoided by proper control of ship's speed, an experienced ship's crew, sufficient crew members on station and good cooperation between ship and tug crew. ( ) The aforementioned situation can get very critical for the tug when the ship is further increasing her speed and as a result overtakes the tug. ( ) A tug's towlines should not be dropped into the water but preferably be lowered onto the tug's deck guided by the ship's crew. ( ) When applicable, Norman pins should be raised on board the tug to prevent the towline slipping along the sides. This avoids the towline fouling the ship or tug propeller. In the case of a fixed pitch propeller the ship's propeller should be stopped when the ship's stern towlines are released.

Questão 295

It is not always easy, or may even be impossible, to vary a towline's length when this would be the best option. This largely depends on the way the towline is secured on board the tug and this in turn depends on the tug's deck equipment, the assistance required and whether ship lines or tug towlines are used. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Method of towing and varying towline lengths” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.7, p.179)

A) Although different methods of towline usage and securing exist, they hardly allow towline length to be varied efficiently unless a towing winch is used. It should be noted carefully that operational safety is involved if towlines cannot be slackened or released in na emergency.

B) When ship lines are used they are often secured to the towing hook. In this case the crew of the tug have to vary the line's length if so required and this may take some time with the shortage of manpower nowadays.

C) The length of a towline secured to a tug's bollard or towing bitt can be changed, although it takes time and manpower to do so and can only be done when the line is slack. In an emergency it is almost impossible to release a towline secured to a towing bitt, whereas when secured to a quick release towing hook, release should not be problematic, provided the release system is reliable.

D) In tugs not provided with a towing winch, towing bitts, bollards and/or quick release hooks are used. Even when equipped with a towing winch, towing bitts and bollards are used when more than one line has to be fastened aboard.

E) Even though not equipped with a towing winch, tugs often use their own towlines. For tugs towing on a line, these are usually of fixed length with an eye spliced at each end. One eye is secured to the ship's bollard and the other to the tug's towing hook. The length of such lines cannot be varied. These tugs often have two (or more) towlines of different fixed lengths.

Questão 303

With respect to towline force control there are some types of towing winches. Are they: (Cap.7, p.183-184)
I. the conventional towing winch: A conventional winch consists of a drum with a brake arrangement on one side and a drive arrangement on the other.
II. the constant tension winch: Constant tension winches are basically a large torque motor driving a drum through large gears. These winches can maintain a relatively constant tension in the towline with slowly varying forces.
III. the render-recovery winch: There are various types of render-recovery winches, but they have all one common feature, that being, the towline force can be regulated by the tug master and set for when the winch should render and for when the winch should recover. Questão 306

Questão 307

A towline must fulfil certain basic functions. Firstly to function as the load carrying link between tug and ship and secondly to cope with dynamic loads resulting from relative motion between tug and ship. This leads to basic requirements for towlines for harbour tugs. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Towlines requirements” and mark true (T) or false (F): (Cap.7, p.188) ( ) Tensile strength: A towline should be of sufficient strength to cope with the forces that can be experienced during shiphandling operations. ( ) Elongation: Dynamic loads in a towline should be well compensated for in order to avoid excessive loads in the line, on the winch and fairleads or other methods must be used to avoid peak loads. ( ) Linear density: A towline should be manageable on board a tug as well as on board a ship. When no towing winch is used a towline should be flexible enough for easy handling. ( ) Life: When in use a towline should suffer a minimum of wear, distortion and loss of strength, providing as long a life as possible.

Questão 308

A steel wire rope consists of a number of strands (pernas) wound (enrolados) around a central core (madre) of fibre or wire. Each strand in turn consists of a number of wires (fios ou arames) wound to form a strand. Wire ropes are constructed in various ways. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding “Steel wire ropes - Constructions” and mark true (T) or false (F): (Cap.7, p.189) ( ) Lay: The twisting of strands to form a rope, or wires to form a strand, during manufacture. ( ) Right hand or left hand lay: The angle or direction of strands relative to the centre of a rope. When looking along the line of the rope and the direction of the strands is clockwise it is called left hand lay. If the direction is anti-clockwise it is called right hand lay. ( ) Cross lay and equal lay: Terms describing the lay of wires used to make up strands. In a cross lay strand all wires have a different lay length. High stress concentration at the cross-over points leads to early internal failure. Equal lay wire ropes tend to last longer, mainly due to less internal wear. They also withstand cyclic loading better and are stronger. There are a number of constructions available for equal lay strands and the most suitable is the Warrington/Seale construction. ( ) Lang's lay: A method of making a rope where the lay of wires in a strand is the same as the lay of strands in the rope. It has worse wearing properties than ordinary lay but tends to untwist so has only limited use. ( ) Ordinary lay: A method of making a wire rope where the lay of wires in a strand is opposite to the lay of strands in the rope.

A) (F) (T) (T) (F) (T)

B) (F) (T) (F) (T) (T)

C) (T) (T) (F) (T) (F)

D) (T) (F) (T) (F) (T)

E) (F) (F) (T) (T) (F)

II. Ropes with more wires have greater flexibility and fatigue resistance but resistance to abrasion is less. Fibre cores allow easier handling and are ideal for use with smaller wire sizes and where wire is to be handled manually.
III. Where steel wire ropes are used on towing winches it is advantageous to use a steel wire core. Wires constructed with a steel wire core offer greater resistance to the crushing forces experienced on winches, are 7-8 per cent stronger and stretch slightly more than a fibre core wire of the same diameter. Questão 314

Due to the increased bollard pull of tugs, the diameter and weight of steel wire towlines has increased. Consequently they are increasingly difficult to handle, not only by a tug's crew but also by the low number of crew members available aboard ships to fasten or release towlines. The different types and constructions of fibre towlines all have their own specific characteristics. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Synthetic fibre ropes – Rope construction” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.7, p.191)

A) Three strand ropes: The three strand rope, or hawser-laid rope, is the most common of twisted ropes. They have a tendency to 'kink' or 'hockle' which significantly reduces strength. The rope has good abrasion resistance.

B) A new type of rope of HMPE fibres is the 12 x 12 strand rope, which consists of 12 individual 12-strand ropes that have been braided together to form the final rope. With this type of rope individual strands can easily be repaired by using traditional splicing methods. Twelve strand ropes of HMPE fibres are, among others, frequently used for towlines, including escort tug towlines.

C) Six strand ropes: Six strand ropes with core are twisted ropes similar to conventional wire ropes. It is not as prone to hockling as a three strand rope.

D) Eight strand ropes: Eight strand plaited ropes, also called square braid, are made up of four pairs of two strands. The pairs of strands are alternately left hand lay and right hand lay. The balance between left and right hand strands makes them virtually unkinkable and very flexible. The rope are less durable than twisted ropes, have a high energy absorption capability, and essentially have the same strength as a three strand rope of the same dimensions.

E) Left hand and right hand lay: The same as with wire ropes. Left hand lay is also called S-lay and right hand lay Z-lay. When holding the rope vertical the direction of the strands corresponds to the diagonal line in the letter S or Z.

Questão 319

The messenger is an important part of the towline. It should float, be long and strong enough and easy to handle. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “The messenger” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.7, p.196)

A) A better method is to make use of a grommet. Take the grommet through the eye of the towline and the messenger line is then girth-hitched to the grommet. The grommet can also be spliced around the eye of the pendant or main towline or messenger line can be spliced directly to the eye of the pendant or main towline. Such systems don't create much pressure on pendant or main towline and result in much less abrasion.

B) Rope diameter should be such that the MBL is more than enough for the weight of towline, even in case of very high ships. Various diameters can be found, but approximately 25 mm may be the best. Due to the low friction, Polypropylene lines should not be used. They will easily slip on the capstan or winch head. HMPE might be the best, because it has a high friction and it floats.

C) Sometimes three strand ropes are used as messenger but this should not be done because a three strand rope will rotate and induce twisting in the pendant and may do so into the main towline as well. In particular this will occur with high ships. Therefore messenger lines should be braided, torque balanced ropes.

D) It has been found that the typical girth hitch connection (which is more or less the same as a cow-hitch connection) method can cause substantial damage to the eye of the pendant or main towline. Under load, the girth hitch bites down on the eye of the towing arrangement, often with intense pressure. Over time, and repeated use, the excessive pressure causes internal abrasion and fibre fusing.

E) If needed a swivel can be included in the messenger line to avoid any twist that might occur.

Questão 326

Consider the following statements: (Cap.7, p.204)
I. Two aspects are important when using steel wire towlines: 'elastic limit' and 'endurance limit'.
II. Steel has some elasticity. This means that under load a steel wire elongates and when the load is removed it returns to its original length. This is only true up to the so-called 'elastic limit', approximately one third of the minimum breaking load of the wire. When load exceeds this limit it results in permanent elongation of the wire.
III. The so-called 'endurance limit', approximately half the minimum breaking load, is also of great influence on the life of a steel wire. Tests have shown that when a steel wire cable has several times endured a load higher than the 'endurance limit' its life is very short and it breaks without ever being exposed to a load up to the 'elastic limit'. It is clear that shock loads play an important role.
IV. Taking into account the towline force of twice the bollard pull of a tug, the minimum breaking strength of a steel wire towline should then be at least four times the bollard pull of the tug, in order to stay within the 'elastic limit' and 'endurance limit'. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), what is the correct alternative?

B) (I), (III) and (IV) solely are true

C) All of them are true

D) (I) and (IV) solely are true

E) (I) and (III) solely are true

Questão 327

Two aspects are important when using steel wire towlines: 'elastic limit' and 'endurance limit'. Steel has some elasticity. This means that under load a steel wire elongates and when the load is removed it returns to its original length. This is only true up to the so-called 'elastic limit', approximately two thirds of the minimum breaking load of the wire. When load exceeds this limit it results in permanent elongation of the wire. The so-called 'endurance limit', approximately half the minimum breaking load, is also of great influence on the life of a steel wire. Tests have shown that when a steel wire cable has several times endured a load higher than the 'endurance limit' its life is very short and it breaks without ever being exposed to a load up to the 'elastic limit'. It is clear that shock loads play an important role. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), which answer best fill in the blanks? (Cap.7, p.204) “Taking into account the towline force of _____ the bollard pull of a tug, the minimum breaking strength of a steel wire towline should then be at least _____ the bollard pull of the tug, in order to stay within the 'elastic limit' and 'endurance limit'.”

Questão 328

A system that has been proposed is an automated hook up system, the Aarts Autohook, made by Aarts Autohook BV, Amsterdam, which also works with a crane. No crew are required on the deck of the ship or tug to secure or release towlines. Securing or releasing the towline can be achieved in a minimum of time and at a rather high ship's speed. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Automatic hook up system” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.7, p.206)

A) At the end of the tug's towline a simple ball is fastened, the connector. The connector is placed by a specially designed tug's crane, the manipulator, in a hook-up point aboard the ship to be assisted. The crane is controlled from the wheelhouse. The system can be used, for instance, at terminals where the same ships call regularly, because in order to use the system the ships must be fitted with a number of these hook-up points at convenient locations for the tug assistance required.

B) Controlled placing of the connector by the manipulator in the hook-up points of a ship having a rather high speed could be difficult at night, in reduced visibility or in wave and swell conditions, particularly near the shoulders. At the stern of the ship it should be easier. Problems may arise when a line breaks, though wear will be less because the towlines do not pass through ship's fairleads.

C) The system can be fitted to any type of tug. The Triple A design concerns harbour and terminal tugs, whether stern driven or tractor type. The Triple E type is also equipped for escorting, emergency towing and emergency response duties, such as fire-fighting and oil spill control.

D) The deck mounted and/or hull mounted hook-up points must also be placed in such a way that the ship can be handled in a loaded as well as in a ballested condition.

E) There were two types of hook-up points: active and passive. Connection and disconnection to active points is by means of the manipulator. For disconnecting from the passive points there are two possibilities, either by manipulator or from the ship by remote control from the wheelhouse or locally, activating a hydraulic cylinder which lifts the connector out of the hook-up point.

Questão 330

Guidance on shipboard towing and mooring equipment for ships constructed on or after 2024 is published by MSC.1/ Circ. 1175/Rev.1. This guidance will take effect once the relevant SOLAS regulation enters into force on the 1 January 2024. The revised guidance does not supersede the Guidance on shipboard towing and mooring equipment (MSC/Circ.1175) which remains applicable to ships constructed on or after 1 January 2007 but before 1 January 2024. The IMO circular provides standards for the design and constructions of shipboard fittings and supporting hull structures for both towing methods (Normal towing; and Other towing) and is not applicable to shipboard fitting and supporting hull structure for: (Cap.7, p.209) 1. towing operations necessary for manoeuvring in ports and sheltered waters associated with the normal operations of ships. 2. escort towing. 3. canal transit towing. 4. emergency towing. B) (2), (3) and (4)

C) (1), (3) and (4)

D) (1), (2), (3) and (4)

E) (1) and (3)

Questão 331

TOW is the safe towing load which means the safe load limit of shipboard fittings used for normal and other towing. TOW used for ‘normal towing operations’ and ‘other towing services’ should not exceed 80% of the design loads, and for fittings used for both the greater of the safe towing loads. TOW, in tons, of each shipboard fitting should not exceed 80% of the design towing load and be marked (by weld bead or equivalent) on the fittings intended for towing. For fittings to be used for both, towing and mooring, to be marked with SWL in tons/tons for mooring and TOW for towing. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), the provisions on TOW as mentioned above apply for the use of no more than: (Cap.7, p.209)

A) five towing lines.

B) four towing lines.

C) three towing lines.

D) two towing lines.

E) one towing line.

Questão 336

SOLAS regulations require that from 1 January 2012, all vessels should have a ship-specific emergency towing procedure. However, emergency towing arrangements are required only on tank vessels, which includes oil tankers, chemical tankers and (liquid) gas carriers of not less than 20,000 tonnes deadweight. For all other ships such a system is not yet required, although these ships can become disabled too, for instance due to loss of propulsion which happens with bulk carriers, container vessels and ro-ro vessels in particular. There are a variety of prefabricated emergency towing systems available on the market that satisfy IMO requirements. Recently, in a response to a request from the Alaska Maritime Prevention & Response Network, Seattle-based engineering firm, Glosten, with support from Samson Rope Technologies, developed a new emergency towing system called the Emergency Vessel Attachment and Towing System (EVATSTM), which might be a solution for all ships. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “EVATS” and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.7, p.212-213)

A) EVATS can also be deployed from a towing vessel to a disabled ship using conventional methods. The two standard EVATS designs (300 MT and 411 MT) exceed the strength requirements established by SOLAS for emergency towing systems.

B) It is intended to be deployed from a disabled ship into the water, where the towing end is then recovered from the surface. The system can be mobilised ashore and delivered by helicopter, or carried onboard the ship.

C) EVATS is a synthetic HMPE system designed to connect a towline to multiple attachment points on a disabled ship.

D) Deployment and attachment cannot be carried out when the disabled ship is in a blackout situation with no power on deck.

E) Depending on ship size, foredeck arrangement, loaded condition (ie freeboard), and weather and sea conditions, it is estimated that two to four crewmembers can rig and deploy the system when stored onboard or delivered onboard by helicopter. In the event of tug-to-ship deployment, the same number of crewmembers would be needed to manually lift the bridle from the deck of the tug and rig the system for use.

Questão 340

In the example (figure), a loaded tanker underway at speed has an engine failure. The ship veers to starboard, which cannot be stopped by the ship's rudder. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements and mark true (T) or false (F): (Cap.9, p.247-249) ( ) tug nº 1 is not in a position to counteract the sheer effectively, but the position of tug nº 2 is much more effective. The same applies for the rudder tug nº3. Effectiveness of the rudder tug in applying steering forces does not differ much from a tug at ship's side with lines secured. In wave conditions the effectiveness of a both tugs declines fast. The most significant difference with tug nº1 is not only that the rudder tug is in an effective position, but is able to apply steering forces to starboard as well as to port. ( ) Regarding tug nº1 it should be kept in mind that this tug might even have an opposite effect. If the ship veers to port instead of starboard, tugs nos 2 and 3 are ineffective in braking the sheer. If tugs are secured at a ship's side in order to anticipate failure they are needed on both sides, or at least a rudder tug should be used, provided the tug is sufficiently powerful. ( ) When the tugs at a ship's side have a bowline they can apply braking forces as well as steering forces. Tugs at the ship's side applying braking forces also create a turning moment. This is another reason why tugs are needed at both sides. A rudder tug can apply braking forces without creating a high turning moment. ( ) There is another aspect to be taken into account, which could be important, for instance, in situations involving partly loaded tankers and strong beam winds. Although tug nos 2 and 3 are trying to stop the sheer, they will push the ship, together with the wind, into the direction of the dangerous area, while tug nº1 is pushing in a safer direction. ( ) When a conventional tug is used aft instead of an omnidirectional tug, ship's speed should be low - say a maximum of four to five knots - to permit control of the vessel in case of a failure. Conventional tugs aft can only apply braking forces and steering forces to both sides at a very low ship's speed, while a conventional tug forward cannot apply any braking forces.

Questão 349

Escort tugs have to deliver steering and/ or braking forces in case of emergency. Steering forces are considered to be particularly important. That is true as long as there is sufficient room ahead and bends to be navigated are not too sharp. In that case a ship can be steered and kept free from dangerous areas. However, it depends on a number of factors whether the steering assistance of an escort tug will be sufficient to keep a ship in safe waters. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements regarding to “Braking and steering forces” and mark true (T) or false (F): (Cap.9, p.253-255) ( ) When an engine or steering failure happens while the manoeuvring area or distance ahead is very restricted, braking power is required. The most effective means to take way off, provided there is sufficient room, is to initiate a turn. This has the effect of slowing down the tanker or gas carrier qickly and reducing head reach. ( ) In most cases, if circumstances allow, it is better to assist the escorted tanker to turn, for instance, 180º or 360º, particularly at higher escort speeds. It should, however, be noticed that assisting tanker turns at relatively high speeds imposes high loads on the tug (and tanker) and may be unsafe, as tug speed will increase appreciably above tanker speed when on the 'outside' of the turn. ( ) For navigating a not too sharp bend at 10 knots speed, which means often a telegraph setting of half speed, or full maneuvering speed, a rudder angle of 30 degrees, can normally be regarded more or less as a maximun. ( ) Rudder forces on a ship with the rudder blocked at a certain rudder angle are reduced when the propeller is stopped, and in case of a controllable pitch propeller, when pitch is set for zero. It enlarges the possibilities for an escort tug to steer the tanker. Det Norske Veritas assumes the rudder lift forces without propeller turning to be 0.53 times the forces with the propeller turning. If circumstances allow the ship could also be stopped by using its engine and with the assistance of the escort tug. ( ) Maximum steering and braking forces a local escort tug should be able to apply should be based on a study of failure scenarios representative of the ships and areas concerned, including the local situation and circumstances. ( ) It can be expected that for the given failure scenarios, a untethered escort tug can react faster and consequently needs to apply relatively lower steering forces than when passive escorting is utilised. In that case there is a much larger time delay before an escort tug can be effective.

A) (T) (F) (T) (F) (T) (T)

B) (T) (T) (F) (T) (T) (F)

C) (F) (F) (T) (F) (F) (T)

D) (F) (T) (F) (T) (T) (F)

E) (F) (T) (T) (T) (F) (F)

Questão 355

The table with permissible values of heeling angle and steady towline force as function of loading condition and escort speed is to be displayed in the wheelhouse next to the control desk or another appropriate location. Robert Allan Ltd developed an Escort Safety Placard of which an example is shown below. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), consider the following statements and choose the incorrect alternative: (Cap.9, p.257)

A) In the tug specific placard is shown a Rated Escort Speed of 10 knots which can be compared with the rated escort speed of an escort class notation. It should, however, be well understood that the heeling angle limits apply to all speeds.

B) As the heeling angles depend on stability and stability on the loading condition, the heeling angles depend on the loading condition. Therefore, the placard shows the heeling angles for the two ‘extreme’ loading conditions covered in the tug’s trim and stability book, although limits of heeling angles of the two loading conditions do not differ much.

C) The simulations demonstrated situations and manoeuvres which are extremely risky due to too high heeling angles that could be reached at 10 knots speed. In particular the manoeuvre from delivering maximum steering forces to starboard towards delivering maximum steering forces to port and vice versa is a high-risk manoeuvre. This not applied if a tug is equipped with an active winch. Risks become low when such manoeuvres are performed when operating on the winch brake.

D) For the placard a suitable inclinometer is required. Therefore, to show the heeling angle with sufficient precision, an electric type of inclinometer is needed. Such an inclinometer, e.g. Daniamant DanEl-300, can be customized for an escort tug and heeling zones can be programmed, while also visual and/or audible alarms are possible.

E) The tug specific coloured sections green, amber and red show the safe, cautious and critical heeling angles and their limits. In the ‘caution’ zone, the tug master must take actions to reduce the heeling angle, while in the red zone immediately action is required.

Questão 363

This design has become reality, Tugs TP I and TP II had been built and operated until 2015 at the coal port at Ridley Island, Canada. They had two azimuth propellers in line, one forward and one aft. The idea of building this type of tug was developed after two platforms powered by a 3,600 hp diesel driving two azimuth thrusters came onto the market for sale. Bollard pull ahead, astern and sideways is almost the same. When thrusters operate in line there is loss of thruster efficiency. In theory this gives loss of about 6 per cent. The two tugs have been a cost effective investment. Their total cost was less than two thirds of the cost of one comparable traditional tug with azimuth thrusters. They are specific ship docking tugs and operated for ships arriving at the coal terminal in the push-pull mode often parallel to the ship with a towline from the tug's bow secured to the ship. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), the tugs operate very successfully at the coal terminal and, according to the owners, could safely handle vessels in the 180,000-200,000 dwt range: (Cap.10, p.273-274)

A) The Optimum Harbour Tug: The Supertug (1986)

B) The Towliner (1995)

C) Novel New Tractor Tug Design, 1984 (CAN)

D) Tug OMNI 2000 (1994)

E) Compact Tugs

Questão 372

A number of specific tug factors are being found to be crucial for the safe and effective working of an unmanned or autonomous tug: (Cap.10, p.283)
I. The tug should be able to deliver pushing and pulling forces at a ship having speed and when stopped in the water. Pushing forces are not at problem, but pulling forces for instance to keep a berthing ship up against the onshore wind requires the tug to be made fast to the ship.
II. The tug should be able to pull with the propulsion units at a distance from the ship hull, large enough to avoid too much loss in pulling power.
III. The tug should be able to oppose the interaction suction/pressure wave forces and turning moments when operating in close distance to the ship, especially near the bow.
IV. The tug should be able to deliver forces that counteract the forces of wind, current and waves and should be able to deliver the required steering and braking forces.
V. The tug should be able to assist all kind of ships, rigs, oil platforms, FPSOs, FLNGs, etc., all with their specific behaviours and dimensions. VI. The tug should be able to berth and unberth. Questão 373

The Ramora 2400 (a completely worked-out idea of an unmanned tug) has been conceived to address aspects of ship handling that pose the highest risks to crews, like those of working under the bow. It is designed to be ideally suited to riskier tug tasks or hazardous environments, a reason why experienced tug masters should handle the tug. It is not intended to replace a crewed tug for the vast majority of ship handling operations. RAmora will depend on the situational awareness and skills of an experienced (and specially trained) tug master in working communication with other tugs, pilots and harbour controller. The tug has an all-around fendering, no deckhouse and wheelhouse and the winch is housed within an open ended house toward the RAmora’s bow. A crane is used to pass the towline. Questão 376

A risk management framework includes all componentes and arrangements to design, implement, monitor, review and continually improve the risk management throughout the organisation (ISO31000:2009). The framework depicts what type of risk assessment tools should be used and at what levels in the organisation. These vary from risk assessments on a corporate level to operational risk assessments, and from context analyses conducted at top management level to Last Minute Risk Assessments on board the tug. With the risk management framework determining when and what risk assessment should be done, and the risk matrices showing the risk attitude of the organisation, the assessing of risks can start. According to Capt. Henk Hensen, in the book “Tug Use in Port” (4th Ed.: 2021), the process of risk assessment involves three steps, to be known as: (Cap.11, p.294)

A) Simulation; Analysis; and Evaluation.

B) Criterion; Analysis; and Evaluation.

C) Simulation; Criterion; and Evaluation.

D) Identification; Evaluation; and Criterion.

E) Identification; Analysis; and Evaluation.

Questão 379

A estabilidade de um navio pode ser expressa de diferentes maneiras. Além disso, no mundo marítimo, o termo estabilidade também é usado de várias maneiras - Como por exemplo, em relação à estabilidade de rumo. A estabilidade longitudinal tem aplicação menos prática no trabalho de rebocador. No entanto, a estabilidade transversal do navio é mais relevante e pode ter um efeito dramático nas operações de reboque. De acordo com o contido no livro “Estabilidade dos rebocadores: um guia prático para operações seguras” (HENSEN, Henk; VAN DER LAAN, Markus. Tradução de Plínio Calenzo - FGMar, 2022), considere as seguintes afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção incorreta: (Cap.2, p.10-12) Obs: o rebocador está em condições operacionais normais, sua a integridade do casco não foi violada e não há embarque de água em nenhum compartimento.

A) O GM inicial (altura metacêntrica) em um navio é visto como o principal critério para a estabilidade da embarcação, entretanto este não é o caso para um rebocador. Embora o GM seja importante, é todo o gráfico de estabilidade, incluindo o ângulo de alagamento que realmente mostra a estabilidade do rebocador.

B) Deve-se ter em mente que um determinado GM e gráfico de estabilidade se aplicam apenas àquelas condições de carregamento específicas - por exemplo, com todos os tanques cheios. Outras condições de carregamento resultam em diferentes valores de GM e estabilidade.

C) A respeito da estabilidade transversal, podem ser especificadas as seguintes formas : Estabilidade inicial e estática; e, Estabilidade dinâmica. A Estabilidade inicial e estática é o tipo de estabilidade geralmente usado. Já a Estabilidade dinâmica, leva em consideração a energia devida ao movimento de balanço do navio.

D) A estabilidade no plano horizontal é uma forma distinta conhecida como estabilidade de rumo. A estabilidade de rumo também está relacionada à estabilidade transversal, sendo um aspecto importante para a operação de rebocadores modernos.

E) Quando são finalizados os cálculos de estabilidade do navio, com exceção dos consumíveis, durante a singradura a estabilidade do navio não será alterada, permanecendo o mesmo GM (salvo em algumas exceções, como condição severa de mar, banda permanente, etc.), pois as cargas estão contidas e devidamente peadas nos porões e conveses do navio. Entretanto no rebocador, já que o ângulo e força de atuação do cabo de reboque pode se alterar, modifica-se constantemente a condição de estabilidade do rebocador.

Questão 380

De acordo com o contido no livro “Estabilidade dos rebocadores: um guia prático para operações seguras” (HENSEN, Henk; VAN DER LAAN, Markus. Tradução de Plínio Calenzo - FGMar, 2022), observe a figura baixo, considere as seguintes afirmativas relacionadas à estabilidade inicial GM e marque verdadeiro (V) ou falso (F): (Cap.2, p.11-12) ( ) O centro de gravidade 'G' é o ponto através do qual o navio exerce uma força na direção vertical para baixo. ( ) A localização de G varia de acordo com a quantidade de combustível e água nos tanques, independente de qualquer carga de convés ser transportada e a quantidade de provisões a bordo. Pode-se observar que existem duas forças iguais e que se opõem 'W' (empuxo e peso do navio), com um braço 'GZ', trabalhando no navio para trazê-lo de volta à posição vertical. ( ) A distância entre o metacentro M e o centro de gravidade G é a altura metacêntrica 'GM' do rebocador. É evidente que um valor de GM maior gera menos estabilidade. ( ) Um valor maior de GM pode ser alcançado abaixando a posição do centro de gravidade G. Quando G está em uma posição mais alta, por exemplo, devido a tanques vazios no fundo, água no convés ou transporte de carga pesada no convés, o valor de GM se reduz e pode até se tornar negativo, tendo como resultado um rebocador instável. Essa situação também pode se desenvolver em condições de gelo quando a água do mar congela nos conveses, mastros e superestrutura, reduzindo rapidamente o valor de GM e tornando a situação de estabilidade muito crítica. ( ) Altura metacêntrica GM só se aplica para pequenos e grandes ângulos de inclinação, porque a localização do Metacentro inicial M0 é considerada fixa para esses ângulos.

A) (V) (V) (F) (F) (F)

B) (F) (V) (F) (F) (F)

C) (V) (F) (V) (F) (V)

D) (V) (V) (F) (V) (F)

E) (F) (F) (V) (V) (V)

Questão 381

O centro de gravidade 'G' é o ponto através do qual o navio exerce uma força na direção vertical para baixo. A localização de G varia de acordo com a quantidade de combustível e água nos tanques, independente de qualquer carga de convés ser transportada e a quantidade de provisões a bordo. Pode-se observar que existem duas forças iguais e que se opõem 'W' (empuxo e peso do navio), com um braço 'GZ', trabalhando no navio para trazê-lo de volta à posição vertical. De acordo com o contido no livro “Estabilidade dos rebocadores: um guia prático para operações seguras” (HENSEN, Henk; VAN DER LAAN, Markus. Tradução de Plínio Calenzo - FGMar, 2022), considere a afirmativa relacionada à estabilidade inicial GM e assinale a opção que melhor preenche as lacunas abaixo: (Cap.2, p.11-12) Um valor _____ de GM pode ser alcançado abaixando a posição do centro de gravidade G. Quando G está em uma posição mais _____, por exemplo, devido a tanques vazios no fundo, água no convés ou transporte de carga pesada no convés, o valor de GM se _____ e pode até se tornar _____, tendo como resultado um rebocador _____. Essa situação também pode se desenvolver em condições de gelo quando a água do mar congela nos conveses, mastros e superestrutura, _____ rapidamente o valor de GM e tornando a situação de estabilidade muito _____.

A) maior - baixa - eleva - positivo - estável - elevando - favorável

B) menor - alta - eleva - positivo - estável - elevando - favorável

C) maior - baixa - reduz - negativo - instável - reduzindo - crítica

D) maior - alta - reduz - negativo - instável - reduzindo - crítica

E) menor - alta - eleva - negativo - instável - reduzindo - crítica

Questão 382

O braço GZ é o indicador do grau de estabilidade de um rebocador em vários ângulos de inclinação. A localização de G pode ser calculada para qualquer condição de calado, trim e condição de carregamento. A localização de M será então determinada para diferentes ângulos de inclinação com base na localização de B. Isto dará o braço de endireitamento GZ (GZ = GM x seno(a)) para os vários ângulos de banda e podem ser plotados em um gráfico. Esse gráfico mostra a faixa de estabilidade do rebocador para um determinado calado, um trim e uma condição de carregamento. Todas essas informações são de extrema relevância para a segurança e o desempenho de um rebocador. O gráfico de estabilidade mostrado abaixo é um gráfico típico de estabilidade de um rebocador. A curva do braço de endireitamento, desenhada em vermelho, detalha informações adicionais interessantes. . A curva cresce quase linearmente com o aumento do ângulo de inclinação até a borda do convés imergir. Depois que a borda do convés é imersa, a curva aumenta mais lentamente e aumenta apenas marginalmente. O braço de endireitamento GZ pode ser observado para vários ângulos de inclinação. Por exemplo, a determinado grau de ângulo de adernamento, seguindo a seta preta e, na escala esquerda, é dado o braço de endireitamento. Isso pode ser em metros ou pés. De acordo com o contido no livro “Estabilidade dos rebocadores: um guia prático para operações seguras” (HENSEN, Henk; VAN DER LAAN, Markus. Tradução de Plínio Calenzo - FGMar, 2022), considere a afirmativa relacionada à estabilidade estática e assinale a opção que melhor preenche as lacunas abaixo: (Cap.2, p.14) O topo da curva, com aproximadamente __(1)__ de inclinação, mostra onde o braço de endireitamento e, portanto, o binário de endireitamento W x GZ (deslocamento x GZ) é maior. Depois desse ponto, a estabilidade diminui. A cerca de __(2)__, a água embarcará para o interior do rebocador através das aberturas da praça de máquinas, etc. Isso é chamado de ângulo de alagamento: um ponto importante no qual a estabilidade será reduzida ainda mais até se tornar negativa aproximadamente a __(3)__. O rebocador então emborcará. Onde a linha preta tangente à curva do braço de endireitamento para pequenos ângulos cruza a linha preta vertical em __(4)__, mostra o valor do GM inicial que é a medida da estabilidade inicial.

A) 45 graus – 57,3 graus - 75 graus – 28,6 graus (meio radiano)

B) 45 graus - 50 graus - 65 graus - 57,3 graus (um radiano)

C) 33 graus - 57,3 graus - 65 graus - 28,6 graus (meio radiano)

D) 15 graus - 33 graus - 75 graus - 57,3 graus (um radiano)

E) 33 graus - 50 graus - 75 graus - 57,3 graus (um radiano)

Questão 383

A superestrutura do rebocador varia conforme o projeto do rebocador e pode desempenhar um papel diferenciado caso o rebocador adquira uma banda maior. . De acordo com o contido no livro “Estabilidade dos rebocadores: um guia prático para operações seguras” (HENSEN, Henk; VAN DER LAAN, Markus. Tradução de Plínio Calenzo - FGMar, 2022), considere as seguintes afirmativas, identifique as verdadeiras e assinale a opção correta: (Cap.2, p.15)
I. A reserva de flutuabilidade do convés elevado e da superestrutura aumentará a capacidade de endireitamento do rebocador, se todas as aberturas estiverem fechadas. Como tal, eles formam uma parte importante da estabilidade total que atua contra o emborcamento.
II. O gráfico mostra que, após aproximadamente 20 graus de inclinação, esse castelo de proa torna-se importante auxiliando o braço de endireitamento do rebocador. Porém, há um efeito negativo na força do braço de endireitamento assim como na faixa de estabilidade, que aumenta até aproximadamente 70 graus. Se a superestrutura também for levada em consideração, a faixa de instabilidade aumenta ainda mais até aproximadamente 80 graus, mas somente se todas as aberturas estiverem fechadas.
III. O convés de proa elevado e a superestrutura têm um grande efeito de endireitamento em caso de ângulos de inclinação extremos.
IV. De acordo com padrões normais, a flutuabilidade da área da superestrutura D não está incluída nos gráficos de estabilidade.

A) Todas as afirmativas são verdadeiras

B) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras

C) Apenas as afirmativas (III) e (IV) são verdadeiras

D) Apenas as afirmativas (I) e (III) são verdadeiras

E) Apenas as afirmativas (I), (II) e (IV) são verdadeiras
